# Where we diverge from the sources

Every place this repo knowingly does something different from the work it follows (FidelityFX SSSR, idTech 8, three.js #35046, three r187 PMREM, Frostbite/Lagarde, Lumen, ...). Each entry records what the source does, what we do, why, and what would make us revisit. Nothing here is necessarily wrong. It is a list to come back to.

**Adding an entry:** take the next id. Keep it short: the source's behaviour, ours, the reason, and a trigger to revisit. If an entry is resolved (we converge, or a measurement settles it), set the status and link the commit or doc. Don't delete entries.

Status: `open` (diverges, unexamined) · `measured` (diverges, numbers justify it) · `planned` (we intend to converge) · `resolved` (converged or retired).

| id | area | source does | we do | why | revisit when | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | probes / storage | idTech 8 and three #35046 (Ben's fork) store probes as cube-map arrays (`texture_cube_array`). | A 2D array of 6 × N layers, with the face picked in the shader. | three can't bind `texture_cube_array` (true on r186 and on r187, the floor since 2026-11-10). | three ships #35053 (CubeArrayTexture), or the core path binds cube arrays directly (raw WebGPU can). Phase 1 bench measures `cube-hw`. | resolved for the three path when the cube-array shim is active (W20: `installCubeArraySupport` + `layout: 'auto'` bakes a real `texture_cube_array`, matches the hw-cube reference at max 0 / 255); open for three without the shim (fallback 2D array); resolved for the core path (W7: `ProbeSet` defaults to `cube-hw`) |
| D2 | probes / seams | Hardware cube sampling is seamless; no source warps texels. | `seamFix: 'warp'` centres edge texels on the cube edges, then does 2 bilinear samples. | Needed only because of D1: the 2D-array layout loses the hardware's seamless filtering. Measured at mean 0.05 / 255, max 3 / 255 vs `cube-hw`. | D1 resolves. | measured; resolved where D1 is (W20: on cube arrays `seamFix` is a no-op, `bakedSeamFix` 'none'; 'warp' stays the 2D-array fallback's default) |
| D3 | probes / prefilter | idTech 8 uses its own GGX baker. UE maps roughness to mip by `1 − 1.2·log2(r)` from the 1×1 mip (unverified, see sources.md S12); Frostbite (Lagarde 2014) by `sqrt(α)`-based lod. | three r187's PMREM plan: lod 0 copy, 256-sample filtered importance sampling, last three levels as full 16 px integrals, `lod = maxLod · r · (2 − r)`. | Matches what three users already see from `scene.environment`, so probes and PMREM agree. | The phase 1 brute-force reference shows the r187 mapping's error at some roughness is clearly worse than another mapping. | decided 2026-11-11, done (W31): the default is `lodMapping: 'alpha'` in core and three (`ReflectionProbes.lodMapping`), which blends linearly in r² only between mips both below r = 0.3 and is r187's mapping above; `'r187'` keeps r187's everywhere. Parity checks against the pre-phase-2 node, the 'hw' sampler and `--compare` pin `'r187'` |
| D4 | probes / format | idTech 8 ships BC6H cube arrays. | `rgba16float` by default; BC6H opt-in for baked probes if the phase 1 gate passes. | No compressed HDR format on mobile WebGPU; compute can't write BC6H directly. | Phase 1 format axis results. | open; decided 2026-11-11: stays opt-in. If used, keep the roughest mips uncompressed (the split in `docs/research/probe-layouts.md`, which passes the gate); `rgb9e5ufloat` is the recommended half-memory format |
| D5 | probes / layout | idTech 8 uses octahedral maps for *irradiance* volumes only; reflection probes are cubes. | We test octahedral *reflection* probes (`octa`, maybe `octa-hemi`). | One layout every engine can sample, and no cube arrays needed. | Phase 1 gate. | measured (phase 1, `docs/research/probe-layouts.md`: `octa` is an option; fails the gate at the mirror mip at equal memory, passes on cost) |
| D6 | probes / re-fit | idTech 8 normalizes probe radiance by `frameIrradiance / probeIrradiance` against its own irradiance volumes. | Same formula, with `probeIrradiance` as the capture's L1 SH (Lazarov 2013 uses SH9 at the normal; L1 is our choice, see D60) and `frameIrradiance` from whatever GI the app has (LightProbeGrid, ddgi, lightmap, ...). | We don't own a GI system. | The re-fit lands (phase 2) and is compared on the rooms. | decided 2026-11-11, done (W31, D803): on when the app passes frame irradiance, off otherwise |
| D7 | SSSR / work lists | Plain FFX SSSR has one compaction (classify → ray list); a miss blends to the environment inline. FFX's `samples/hybridreflections` (SDK v1.1.4) adds a second: low-confidence pixels go to a hardware ray list. | The same second compaction (the **miss list**), plus a stored direction and a per-tile cap. See D40. | The hybrid only knows screen space failed after intersect. | Phase 4 measures it against tracing every glossy pixel. | resolved: the same miss list as D40 (the pattern is AMD's, R2); the open question (stored vs regenerated direction) is tracked in D40 / D907 |
| D8 | SSSR→RT / ray origin | Hybrid engines (Pica Pica, idTech 8) vary; some continue from where the march stopped. | The traced ray starts at the surface (offset along the normal) with the same direction the march used. | The march's free-space assumption only holds where it found a hit. | An off-screen scenario shows a measurable cost or quality difference. | done (W8: the traced pass restarts at the surface, origin + N x 3e-3 x view distance) |
| D9 | SSSR→RT / budget | FFX has no ray budget; idTech traces at ½ or ¼ resolution. | `maxTracedRaysPerFrame`, applied per 8×8 tile with per-tile atomic counters. | Atomic append order isn't spatially fair; a global cap starves the same region every frame. | Phase 4. | done (W8, D904; `pnpm check --only ssr-rt` checks the cap) |
| D10 | SSSR / wave ops | FFX assumes wave intrinsics (ballot, prefix count, quad reads, SPD wave reductions). | A subgroup variant, plus a workgroup-memory variant for devices without `subgroups`. Both must match bit for bit after sorting. | WebGPU's `subgroups` is optional. | Never, unless `subgroups` becomes core. | resolved (D300, D301: subgroup and workgroup-memory variants; lists and images identical at occupancy 0) |
| D11 | RT / hit shading | Lumen shades hits from a surface cache; idTech 8 from its GI and lights. | Hits are shaded by the probes at the hit point plus the engine's cheap direct term. No radiance cache. | Out of scope until the hybrid is measured. | Phase 5 results show hit shading as the main error. | done: probes at the hit for three (W28, D901) and core (`probeHitShading`, or W7's `probeRadianceFallback` + `fallbackHitShading`), shadowed directional lights (W25, D903) |
| D20 | probes / node output | #35046 head (`77bbaaaa05`, 2026-11-09): `reflectionProbes()` returns `vec4` (conditional radiance, coverage), no environment slot; the app composes the fallback. | `reflectionProbes()` keeps `vec3` with the environment fallback (the 2026-10-16 to 10-22 PR behaviour). The coverage form landed as `reflectionProbesLocal()` / `sampleReflectionProbesLocal()`: `vec4` (conditional radiance, coverage), no fallback, same maths as head; checked (alpha 0 outside, 1 inside, 0.2 in the fade; rgb·a = vec3 form to 1.5e-8) | r186 has no `indirectSpecular()` provider chain; `scene.environmentNode` is the only hook. | The coverage node lands, or #35046 merges and the floor moves to a release with providers. | measured (W5 `0316cbe`) |
| D21 | probes / offsets | #35046 `projectionOffset`: capture at the probe, projection box centred at probe + offset. | Both: `projectionOffset` as in #35046 (box moves, capture stays), and `originOffset` kept as an extension (capture moves, volumes stay). Checked: offset O at X = originOffset −O at X+O (mean 0.006/255) | Predates the PR's offset (10-19 vs 10-21); same goal, opposite choice of what moves. | `projectionOffset` is added; decide whether to keep `originOffset`. | resolved (W5 `0316cbe`) |
| D22 | probes / layout | A (`77bbaaaa05`): `probes.length` cubes, no environment slot. C (interior): environment at slot 0, probe `i` at `i + 1`. Ben's 10-16 to 10-21 PR: environment last. | Environment last (`probes.length`), fixed so any layout code that hardcodes the slot index agrees with the fork. | Matches the version we ported and the maintainer's fork. | D20 resolves (the slot may go away). | open |
| D23 | probes / bake defaults | `resolution` default 256, floored to a power of two, minimum 256 (PMREM's minimum). | Default 128, rounded down to a power of two (like #35046 `2712ba1b23`), minimum 64 (not 256) | Our prefilter has no 256 floor; 128 is enough for the demos. | The phase 1 bench settles the right default; or rounding replaces the throw. | decided 2026-11-11, done (W31): default 256 in three's `bake()` and core's `ProbeSet.sourceSize`; minimum 64 and power-of-two rounding unchanged; sizing note on `BakeOptions.resolution` (256 mirror-grade, 128 many probes / mobile). Bake, 3 probes: 2.0 -> 4.0 ms. Sizing by probe count still follows the many-small vs few-large measurement (HANDOFF backlog). examples/ssr and examples/showcase still pin 128 in their own code. W60: a probe can now take its own size over the set's (`ReflectionProbe.resolution`, D4700) |
| D24 | probes / capture path | `PMREMGenerator.fromScene` per probe, then `copyTextureToTexture` per mip, new target each bake. | `CubeCamera` + core `ProbePrefilter` straight into the array layers; target, cube target and prefilter reused between bakes. | r186's PMREM writes the CubeUV atlas (see D3); reuse avoids allocation in live capture. | Never for the reuse; the prefilter path follows D3. | open |
| D25 | probes / bounces | C (interior): `bake({ bounces, usePrevious })` re-captures with the previous pass's probes visible. | Not implemented; captures see the environment only (`capturing` gates local probes). | A dropped it; C's version needs r187-dev lighting-context hooks. | The rooms demo shows missing interreflection is the main error. | measured (W31): `bake({ bounces: n })` without three's hooks (D3200); 1 -> 2 bounces on the rooms: mean 5.6-6.4, p99 42-49 / 255, +11 % luminance; it is also the main cause of the SSR-to-probe radiance step on mirrorWall (D3201). Default stays 1. **W37: default 2 (maintainer Q1, D4300)** |

| D27 | probes / live capture | #35046 has no time slicing or environment version tracking; the fork has both (`37bf331235`, `351167b7d1`). | Both, from the fork: `bake({ probes })` (ignored on a layout change), environment captured first and only when `scene.environment` or its `version`/`pmremVersion` changed; plus `environment: 'always'` for render-target environments. Checked: 3 slices = full bake (0/255) | Recommendations 5. | Recommendation 5 lands. | resolved (W5 `0316cbe`) |
| D500 | probes / uniforms | #35046 (all versions) keeps the node's uniforms in three's default per-object group. | All probe uniforms in the shared `renderGroup`. | On r186 and r187 the material observer re-uploads per-object uniforms only for render objects it sees change: moving a probe or toggling `boxProjection` left static meshes unchanged (identical PNG). Upstream likely has the same issue. | three refreshes node uniforms of unchanged render objects, or #35046 fixes it differently. | measured (W5 `0316cbe`) |
| D501 | probes / texture swap | #35046 head switches `_array.value` in `updateBefore` and rebuilds when the probe count changes. | Same, plus the texture id in `customCacheKey` and `bake()` bumping every tracked node (`ReflectionProbes.track`, WeakRef) when the layout changes. | `updateBefore` runs after render objects are fetched, so a static mesh drew one frame with the destroyed array ("Destroyed texture used in a submit" after a resolution change). | Same trigger as D500. | measured (W5 `0316cbe`) |
| D502 | probes / bake order | #35046 head: no environment slot. Fork: environment first. Our port: environment last. | Environment first (fork), so probe captures in the same bake see the new environment. | A reused array otherwise reflected last bake's environment. | D22. | resolved (W5 `0316cbe`) |
| D40 | SSSR / miss list | FFX `hybridreflections` v1.1.4 already appends low-confidence pixels (`confidence < 0.9`) to a deferred HW ray list with a wave atomic and indirect args. | Same pattern, plus a stored direction and a per-8x8-tile cap (D9). Corrects the "FFX has none" wording of D7 and SPEC. | We want the direction and cap; the structure is AMD's. | Phase 4 compares a stored direction against regenerating from the blue-noise index. | open |
| D41 | SSSR / hit validity | Continuous confidence: vignette x squared distance-to-surface falloff, with out-of-frustum, <2 px self-hit, background and back-face rejects; radiance = lerp(env, scene, confidence). | Port as is; the miss-list predicate is `confidence < threshold`. | Binary miss in SPEC loses the soft fade. | Visible popping at the SSR/RT seam. | resolved (D308: the miss list takes `confidence < threshold`, radiance lerps to the fallback by confidence; the composite blends by it, D2400) |
| D42 | SSSR / thickness | `depthBufferThickness` 0.015 view-space; hybrid sample uses 0.2 + 10 r + 0.01 L. | Start at 0.015 constant, add the hybrid terms behind a flag. | Plain sssr has holes on rough surfaces with a fixed value. | Phase 3 scene tests (rough floors). | resolved (D1001: both, hybrid default) |
| D43 | SSSR / roughness space | `roughnessThreshold` 0.2 and mirror test 0.0001 are on alpha (perceptual squared). | `settings.roughnessThreshold` and `missRoughnessThreshold` are perceptual and squared in `writeParams` (the pass boundary); default 0.447 (alpha 0.1998, `DEFAULT_ROUGHNESS_THRESHOLD`). The mirror test and the extracted-roughness output stay alpha. `pnpm check` brackets it: max traced alpha 0.1964 < 0.1998 <= min untraced ramp alpha 0.1999. | Avoids the common mistake of applying 0.2 to perceptual roughness (0.2 alpha = 0.447). | A source we port from uses a different convention. | resolved (W6, perceptual at the API) |
| D44 | SSSR / ray list packing | 15-bit x, 14-bit y, 3 copy flags in a u32. | Same packing (or x,y 14+14 with flags) so `samplesPerQuad < 4` works; denoiser tile list 16+16. | SPEC's 16+16 has no room for quad-copy flags. | Render targets above 16383 px. | resolved (D308: FFX packing, copy flags kept) |
| D45 | SSSR / subgroup quad ops | `QuadReadAcrossX/Y/Diagonal` via `lane ^ 1/2/3`, assumes lanes map to 2x2 quads. | Use `quadSwapX/Y/Diagonal` when available; the workgroup-memory fallback uses explicit tile indexing, not lane arithmetic. | WGSL does not guarantee the lane-to-quad layout for `subgroupShuffleXor` in compute. | A device reports quad ops unsupported but subgroup ops available. | resolved, refined by D300 (workgroup-memory exchange; subgroup ops only for the compaction) |
| D46 | SSSR / variance default | `temporalVarianceGuidedTracingEnabled` true with threshold 0.0 in the sample (range 0–0.01). | Default threshold from the denoiser's variance units; verify what 0.0 does there. | A threshold of 0 traces any pixel with non-zero variance, effectively `samplesPerQuad = 4`. | Phase 3 GPU-ms tests. | open |
| D47 | SSSR / traversal occupancy | `minTraversalOccupancy` 4 exits early when few lanes are alive (wave count); mirrors ignore. | With the workgroup fallback no wave count exists; use workgroup-level active count or drop the exit. | Cannot be ported without `subgroups`. | Measure the cost of dropping it on divergent scenes. | resolved by D301 |
| D48 | SSSR / depth pyramid | 7 levels (mip 0 + 6), r32f, min for forward-Z / max for reverse-Z. | Same; keep `depthInverted` choosing min/max. | | | resolved (D302, then D1600 / D1601: the pyramid is built by `downsampler`) |
| D49 | SSSR / ray sampling | GGX VNDF with blue noise; no mirror-bias knob. | VNDF ported as is; `settings.mirrorBias` (0..1, default 0, W32) is the SSSR15 / PICA bias: Heitz's sampler takes the lobe's spread from U1 (`r = sqrt(U1)`, U2 is the azimuth), whose mirror end is U1 = 0, so `U1 = lerp(u.x, 0, bias)` (D3300). Bias 0 is FFX bit for bit (radiance hash at occupancy 0, roughnessRamp: FFX shader 15750244 = ours unset = ours 0). Measured (examples/ssr `mirrorBiasStats`, 8-bit luma of the final image, still camera, 960x540; bias 0 / 0.25 / 0.5 / 0.75; std = temporal std, added = RMS bias against the same mode's bias-0 mean, noise taken out). Raw: roughnessRamp strips r 0.2 std 10.2 / 6.3 / 4.7 / 3.2, added 0 / 3.6 / 5.3 / 6.7; r 0.3 13.2 / 11.6 / 9.4 / 7.5, 0 / 5.1 / 6.8 / 8.3; mirrorWall floor r 0.2 21.0 / 18.0 / 15.4 / 12.0, 0 / 6.1 / 9.9 / 13.6; r 0.4 20.9 / 21.6 / 21.4 / 20.6, 0 / 3.8 / 6.6 / 9.5. Denoised (FFX): std 0.23-0.48 at every bias, added 0 / 3.8-4.8 / 5.5-7.9 / 7.1-11.6. Full table: `docs/research/mirror-bias-w32.json`. | The knob is cheap and cuts noise (SSSR15 slide 68, PICA p451). With the denoiser on (all three examples' default) the still-camera noise is already under half a level, so the knob only adds bias: the default stays 0. Raw (no denoiser) 0.25-0.5 helps at r <= 0.3 (one-frame RMSE on the ramp at r 0.2: 17.2 -> 12.3 at 0.5) but not near the threshold (mirrorWall r 0.4: 24.1 -> 27.4). | A moving camera or short denoiser history (not measured: the orbit flicker of ssr-bench would show it), or a pipeline without the denoiser. | measured W39 re-measured under @pmndrs/upscaler, still and in a 0.1 deg/frame pendulum orbit, denoiser off / on, after W44 (`docs/research/w39/mirror-bias-upscaler.json`): with the denoiser, rmse vs the raw bias-0 mean rises with bias at r >= 0.2 (ramp moving r .3 8.0 / 10.5 / 13.9 / 18.1 at 0 / 0.25 / 0.5 / 0.75; mirrorWall r .3 8.0 / 9.4 / 11.1 / 13.5), r .1 gains <= 0.2, motion flicker unchanged (d2 0.34-0.52); raw + upscaler: flicker in motion falls (ramp r .2 > 4/255 22.6 -> 13.2%) for more error (9.3 -> 14.9). Default stays 0. |
| D50 | SSSR / resolve | SSSR15 resolves at full res from half-res rays with weight re-normalisation; FFX moved this into its denoiser. | Rely on the `denoiser` seam; no resolve pass in this repo. | Denoising belongs in `denoiser` (CLAUDE.md rule 7). | A resolve-less path (no denoiser) needs a minimal one. | open |
| D51 | hybrid / RT as default | idTech 8 ships RT only for very smooth surfaces and disables it on consoles; FFX's hybrid sample gates RT by confidence and an RT roughness threshold (0.22); probes are the baseline. | RT is optional, off by default, and gated by `rtRoughnessThreshold` + `confidence`. | Matches what shipped; SPEC §5 wording could be read as RT-first. | Phase 4 numbers show RT is cheap enough to default on. | planned; decided 2026-11-11: RT stays opt-in, also at release (low-end by default, opt in to more). Only the provider default changes, to @pmndrs/bvh |
| D52 | hybrid / thresholds | Bands: FFX 0.2 alpha; AMD-GR 0.2/0.6 (assumed perceptual); Lumen 0.3/0.4; HDRP min-smoothness 0.9. Spaces differ. | Three bands in perceptual roughness: mirror, glossy (SSSR + RT), rough (probe), with the cut-offs exposed. Defaults to be chosen at phase 5. | No source is directly comparable. | Phase 5 tuning on the explainer scenarios. | open |
| D53 | hybrid / seam | Lag14 `distance-based roughness` (§4.9.3 p72 Listing 25: `newLinearRoughness = clamp(distIntersectionToShadedPoint / distIntersectionToProbeCenter * linearRoughness, 0, linearRoughness); return lerp(newLinearRoughness, linearRoughness, linearRoughness)`, per local probe from its proxy intersection, App. F) and the roughness-lerp of the corrected direction (App. F Listings F.1/F.2: `localR = lerp(localR, MainR, linearRoughness)`) exist to match SSR and probes; AMD hybrid has none. | **W26:** both in the core WGSL lookup as options (`distanceRoughness`, `roughnessLerp`; core / `ProbeSet` default off), per box-projected volume: t = distance from P to the projection-box hit, d = \|hit - capture point\|, r perceptual (= Frostbite's `linearRoughness`; sources.md's 'alpha' was loose), the volume's lod from r'. Environment slot and unprojected volumes unchanged. three: `ReflectionProbes.distanceRoughness` (**default true**) / `roughnessLerp` (**default false**), set-wide so `reflectionProbes()`, `sampleReflectionProbes()` and the hybrid composite agree (D1501); per-lookup overrides on the node options. Lerp normalized: D2700. | Seam visibility is the main hybrid failure (RTG32, Snow p64). Measured (W26). **Seam metric** (`pnpm check --only seam`, examples/ssr `seamStats`): at confident glossy hits final - lit = w (hit - probe), so its 8-bit luma (8x8 block means, confidence >= 0.9, r 0.05-0.45, denoised) is the step the image makes where confidence fades to the probe; run-to-run <= 0.15. Off -> dist / lerp / both: mirrorWall floor r .1 25.97 -> 26.27 / 26.33 / 26.71, r .2 21.51 -> 21.44 / 21.00 / 21.76, r .3 15.87 -> 15.21 / 13.73 / 13.96, r .4 12.32 -> 11.55 / 9.41 / 9.40; mirrorWall low r .2 21.33 -> 21.02 / 21.18 / 21.22; contact r .2 30.88 -> 30.06 / 30.57 / 30.18; contact low r .3 23.83 -> 23.22 / 23.03 / 22.49; roughnessRamp strips 23.59 -> 22.92 / 23.52 / 22.76; boxRoom r .25 4.61 -> 4.59 / 4.58 / 4.65. **Vs path traced** (showcase compare Measure, 32 spp + OIDN, floors r .25, MAD over glossy px, ours denoised) off / dist / lerp / both: toyCar 16.11 / 15.69 / 16.58 / 16.16, carConcept 20.52 / 20.19 / 21.01 / 20.69, chess 15.00 / 14.63 / 15.47 / 15.11, Sponza 29.76 / 29.74 / 29.55 / 29.53 (probes only moves the same way). Distance roughness: closer to the reference on every stage and a narrower step on 7 of 9 seam cases (+0.3 at r .1, 2x the noise) -> default on. The lerp: the narrowest step at r >= .3 (it pulls the probe toward the blurred average SSR also shows) but farther from the reference on every stage -> default off. Most of the step is not the probe's blur (hit vs probe radiance bias -10 to +23/255; anisotropic stretch SSR has and a probe can't): these options close 4-24 % of it. Lookup cost at 1080p (`--time-lookup`, loop stopped, two runs): cube-array 0.140 / 0.117 ms vs both options 0.160 / 0.130 ms wall, GPU 0.160 / 0.218 vs 0.173 / 0.214 ms: within the run's noise. | A scene where the seam is dominated by blur (hit shading matched first, D903); a sphere proxy (D59) needs its own t; adapters on `ProbeSet` (babylon, pixi) still default off (D2701). | measured (W26: distanceRoughness default on, roughnessLerp opt-in) |
| D54 | hybrid / ray origin | FFX hybrid and Snow-reflections restart at the surface; Lumen and Snow-GI continue the same ray. | Restart at the surface (D8). | See D8. | See D8. | resolved: same as D8 (done, W8) |

WebGPU has no storage/renderable RGB9E5. | Bench `rg11b10ufloat` against `rgba16float` and judge by tone-mapped banding on bright sources. | Source evidence is against rg11b10 for bright HDR. | Phase 1 format axis. | measured (phase 1: no banding on bright sources under the tone map, but a +0.07–0.19 mean offset; `rgb9e5ufloat` is the better 4-byte option) |

| D65 | probes / live update | HDRP time-slices realtime probes over 7 frames; ACS updates 1 cube face per frame; Godot once or every frame. | Whole-probe bake per call (2.2 ms for 3 probes at 128 px). | Simplicity. | Live capture budget exceeded in the rooms. | open |
| D66 | composite / specular occlusion | Lag14 `saturate(pow(NdotV+AO, exp2(-16 r - 1)) - 1 + AO)`; idT8 directional occlusion; Godot flag default on. | Apply the Lag14 term to the probe term only, from the app's AO; SSR/RT carry their own occlusion. | Cheapest sourced option; removes probe light leaking. | Phase 5 leaking in corners. | implemented, opt-in (W7: core `probeSpecularOcclusion`, three `ao` option, a = perceptual²; checked vs CPU 1e-6); W39: where it applies per adapter D4511, examples toggle D4510, measured vs RT and the path tracer D4513; W45: off on traced misses D3551 |
| D67 | composite / DFG form | Lag14 two-channel DFG (f0 and f90); Karis13 scale/bias on F0. | Karis form (f90 = 1) like three. | Matches three's PBR. | Materials with a non-1 f90. | open |
| D68 | denoise seam / demodulation | Snow, ACS, NRD: radiance divided by the pre-integrated BRDF before denoising, multiplied back after; ACS also compresses `x^0.625`. | The seam carries demodulated radiance; compression left to the denoiser. | Keep contracts with `denoiser`. | Denoiser repo decides otherwise. | open |
| D69 | frame contract / scene colour | FFX: current frame, direct only; PICA, SSSR15, AMD-GR: previous frame reprojected; FFX hybrid: either via a flag. | Allow both, default to the current frame lit without specular. | Avoids ghosting and double specular. | The adapter cannot render specular-off. | open |
| D100 | probes / octa layout | Engelhardt & Dachsbacher 2008: octahedral map with the pole on +z; DDGI (Majercik 2019) and idTech 8 tiles: a fixed 1 px border per tile, no mips. | `octa`: pole on +y (sky in the centre diamond), and a gutter that is a fixed *fraction* of the side, half a texel at the last mip (2^(maxLod−m−1) texels at mip m), filled with the wrapped continuation, so one trilinear sample works across mips. | A fixed-pixel border changes the uv mapping per mip, which would need two bilinear samples like `cube2d-warp`. | A layout needs integer gutters (atlas packing), or BC6H blocks straddling the gutter show. | measured (docs/research/probe-layouts.md) |
| D101 | probes / lookups below r = 0.2 | three r187 (D3): `lod = maxLod · r(2 − r)` with trilinear blending between the mirror mip and mip 1. | Same. Measured: at r = 0.05 the blend gives p99 35–46 / 255 against the GGX reference (10× the error at the mips), the same for every layout. | Matches three's PMREM; it is a property of the plan, not of the layout. | Phase 2: try a mapping or an extra mip that spends more resolution below r = 0.2 (D64), and compare with the bench's r < 0.2 rows. | measured (W7: opt-in `lodMapping: 'alpha'`, blend linear in r² between mips below r = 0.3: p99 at r .05 44.5 -> 19.7 (128 px), 35.4 -> 21.9 (256 px); default stays r187. Next: an extra mip); decided 2026-11-11, done (W31): `'alpha'` is the default in core and three. Visible: glossy floors below r .3 reflect sharper (showcase toyCar / chess / carConcept mean 3.4-3.7, p99 37-43 / 255 at 128 px). Next: the extra-mip experiment, possibly on the maintainer's downsampler |
| D102 | probes / reference | Brute-force prefilter references (Karis 2013, Lagarde 2014) integrate the full GGX BRDF for a view direction. | `GGXReference` integrates the GGX lobe with V = N = R over mip 0, the same assumption the prefilter makes, so the bench measures layout, format and plan error, not the split-sum approximation itself. | The question was layout and format, not split-sum error. | Someone needs the split-sum error at grazing angles (a V ≠ N reference). | open |
| D103 | probes / small-float writes | SPEC: rg11b10ufloat "written by a render pass or a copy". | `rg11b10ufloat` and `rgb9e5ufloat` are packed in compute from f16 bits into a buffer, then `copyBufferToTexture`; no `rg11b10ufloat-renderable` needed. Adds about 0.01 ms per probe. | One path for both formats, on every device. | A live (per-frame) probe wants the render-pass write. | measured |
| D104 | bench / lookup timing | SPEC metrics and the measurement rules: GPU ms from timestamp queries. | The lookup is timed by wall clock over bursts of 32 render passes (min of 9), plus a ratio to `cube-hw` from back-to-back burst pairs; bake spans are timestamps bracketed by one-texel passes, min of 9 (not a median of 5). | On Dawn/Metal, timestamps on consecutive render passes overlap (identical passes timed 0.8× by order, spans 2× the wall clock), begin/end-only compute timestamps over many passes lost the device (out of memory), and other agents' GPU work shared the machine. | Timestamps behave on another backend, or the machine is quiet. | measured |
| D200 | encode / BC6H texel fetch | GPURealTimeBC6H gathers each 4×4 block with GatherRed/Green/Blue and a point sampler. | `textureLoad` per texel with coordinates clamped to the mip, so mips under 4 px repeat their edge texels into the block padding. | WGSL has gathers, but clamped loads handle the sub-block mips that probe chains end with, with no sampler. | Never, unless encode time is dominated by fetches. | open |
| D201 | encode / BC6H degenerate blocks | The HLSL divides by the block axis' component sum and by `endpoint1 - endpoint0` unguarded, and `normalize`s zero vectors; a flat block goes through NaN, which D3D hardware usually turns into index 0 (its min/max drop NaN). | Guards: a zero axis becomes (1/3, 1/3, 1/3), coinciding endpoints give index 0, a zero partition axis gives distance-to-point. Inputs are clamped to [0, 65504] and NaN to 0 (ufloat), and `pack2x16float` never sees a value outside f16. | WGSL leaves NaN/overflow through `pack2x16float` and `u32()` indeterminate, so the NaN path isn't portable. | A CPU reference shows different blocks on flat input. | open |
| D202 | encode / BC6H output path | The HLSL writes `RWTexture2D<uint4>` and the app copies it into a BC6H texture (D3D11 CopyResource between compatible formats). | Blocks go to a storage buffer (one `vec4<u32>` per block, rows padded to 256 bytes), then `copyBufferToTexture` per mip. | WebGPU can't alias or copy between an rgba32uint texture and a BC texture, and compute can't write BC. | WebGPU gains texture-to-texture copies between block-compatible formats. | open |
| D203 | encode / BC6H anchor indices | GPURealTimeBC6H swaps a subset's endpoints when its anchor texel's index has the MSB set, then recomputes indices. At r = 0.5 its `ComputeIndex3` gives exactly 4.0 in f32 (8.0 for `ComputeIndex4`) on both sides of the swap, so the anchor keeps a 3-bit value in a 2-bit slot and the MSB lands in the next texel's index (an upstream bug). | After the index loop the anchors are clamped: modes 2/6 to 3 (texel 0 and the subset-1 fixup texel), mode 11 to 7. 3 and 4 (7 and 8) are equally close to r = 0.5. | It corrupted 128 of 7315 two-subset blocks on the procedural 256 cube and 16 of 58173 on random two-subset content (examples/regressions); after the clamp 0. | Upstream fixes it, or a CPU reference decoder lands. | resolved |
| D300 | SSSR / quad copy flags | Classify reads the neighbours' `require_copy` with `WaveReadLaneAt(lane ^ 1/2/3)` after `ffxRemapForWaveReduction`, relying on lanes = local index. | Both variants exchange `require_copy` through a 64-entry workgroup array indexed `lid ^ 1/2/3` (same remap). Subgroup ops are used only for the ray-list compaction (ballot count, exclusive add, elect, broadcast-first). | WGSL ties neither shuffles nor quad ops to `local_invocation_index` in compute, so a lane read is not a pixel read; workgroup memory is exact and keeps the variants bit-identical. Refines D45. | Workgroup-memory exchange shows up in classify's GPU ms. | open |
| D301 | SSSR / traversal occupancy | `minTraversalOccupancy` compares `WaveActiveCountBits(true)` (wave64 preferred) per iteration. | Subgroup variant: ballot count over the subgroup (32 on Apple). Workgroup variant: the march loop runs in uniform control flow and counts active invocations of the 64-wide workgroup each iteration (3 barriers per iteration). Defaults stay 4. | No wave count without `subgroups` (D47). The workgroup count equals FFX on wave64 hardware. Consequence: at occupancy > 0 the two variants' images differ (about 3000 of 518400 px at 960x540; lists still equal), so parity is checked at occupancy 0, which is the hybrid sample's default. | Measure the workgroup variant's loop cost on divergent scenes, or drop the exit there. | measured |
| D302 | SSSR / depth pyramid dispatch | SPD: one dispatch, a global atomic hands the tail mips to the last workgroup; 13 UAV mips bound. | Two dispatches of a 64x64-tile kernel (copy + mips 1-3 from depth, then 4-6 from mip 3), no global atomic, all in `sssr/depthPyramid.ts`. Same format and relation as FFX (D48: r32float, mip 0 = copy, 6 reduced levels, min / max for reverse-Z). | Core WebGPU allows 4 storage textures per stage; mips <= 6 come from one tile, so the atomic is never needed. Min/max is exact, so texels equal SPD's. | The maintainer's `downsampler` replaces this module after the merge. | resolved, superseded by D1600 / D1601 (`downsampler` builds the pyramid) |
| D303 | SSSR / out-of-bounds loads | D3D robust loads return 0 for an out-of-range texel or mip (depth, normal, colour); classify's off-screen threads in edge tiles therefore read depth 0 and count as reflective in `g_TileCount`. | Explicit bounds checks return 0 for depth, normal and colour in intersect. Classify clamps the load and treats off-screen threads as not reflective. | WebGPU clamps (or returns any in-bounds value), which would change ray stalls at the screen edge and the inverted-depth march. The classify change only matters for an edge tile whose on-screen pixels are all rough. | Never, unless edge tiles differ from a reference. See D5160 for the march's depth load (clamped on screen). | measured (march: D5160) |
| D304 | SSSR / output formats | Extracted roughness R8_UNORM; ray length in radiance.w only; blue noise R8G8_UNORM. | Extracted roughness r32float; radiance keeps rgb + ray length in w, plus a separate r32float hit distance that is 0 where confidence is 0 (the denoiser repo's `hitDistance` convention); blue noise rgba8unorm (same unorm8 quantisation). | r8unorm, rg8unorm and r16float aren't core storage formats. The denoiser seam takes a separate hit-distance texture; its type says r16float but it only `textureLoad`s, so r32float binds. | `texture-formats-tier1` is common enough to require, or the denoiser seam pins a format. | open |
| D305 | SSSR / blue-noise tables | The host uploads `sobol_256spp_256d` (256x256 u32) and `scramblingTile` (512x256 u32); the shader XORs them per pixel per frame. | The only entries SSSR reads (sample 0, dimensions 0 and 1) are folded offline into a 128x128x2 byte table (32 KB, base64 in `blueNoiseData.ts`); the per-frame golden-ratio shift stays in the shader. Values are identical. | 1.2 MB of tables for 32 KB of used data. | A consumer needs more samples or dimensions (e.g. multi-spp tracing). | open |
| D306 | SSSR / traversal mip cap | `FFX_SSSR_DEPTH_HIERARCHY_MAX_MIP` 6, constant. | `mipCount - 1`, which is 6 for any target over 64 px. | A smaller pyramid has no mip 6. | Never. | open |
| D307 | SSSR / mirror flag | The tile list carries no flag; the denoiser re-tests `IsMirrorReflection` on the extracted roughness per pixel. | Extracted roughness as FFX, plus a `tileFlags` buffer (u32 per 8x8 tile: bit 0 listed, bit 1 has a non-mirror glossy pixel). Listed without bit 1 = a mirror-only tile the denoiser may skip. | D308 | SSSR / miss list vs hybrid sample | `samples/hybridreflections`: wave32 groups; `do_hw = confidence < 0.9 && !IsBackground(hit.z)` (1e-6 epsilon) and roughness <= 0.22; thickness 0.2 + 10 r + 0.01 L; stores the packed coords only (u32, direction regenerated); writes no radiance for `do_hw` pixels; `prepare_indirect_args_hw` after intersect, 32 per group. | Same predicate and epsilon, with `missRoughnessThreshold` default 1 and SSSR's constant 0.015 thickness (D42). 8-byte entries: FFX-packed coords (copy flags kept) + oct-encoded world direction as 2 x snorm16. Radiance is always written (FFX's lerp to the fallback), so the frame is complete with no RT pass; a traced hit overwrites it. One atomic per subgroup / per workgroup (prefix sum). Own args pass after intersect, 64 per group; counter `[append, this frame, dropped]`. The hybrid's thickness and roughness gate are phase 4 tuning. | Phase 4: compare stored vs regenerated direction; add the per-tile cap. | open (since: the per-tile cap is done in the traced pass, D904 / D1100; the thickness default is the hybrid one, D1001. A `'direct'` variant needs `directSpecular`, which three keeps in an unnamed var (not shared by name like `totalDiffuse`), so it needs a lighting-model hook. | open |
| D401 | frame / motion | FFX takes uv-space motion scaled by `motionVectorScale` (history uv = uv − motion·scale). three's `velocity` and denoiser-core's `FrameGuides.motion` are NDC deltas (current − previous, y up). | Default: uv-space (current − previous, y down) in rg16float, scale [1, 1]. `motion: 'ndc'` stores three's velocity as is, scale [0.5, −0.5]. | One texture that FFX-style passes read without a scale. | The denoise seam lands: if both SSSR and the denoiser read the same motion texture, pick one convention for both. | open |

| D404 | frame / transparency | Deferred engines (and FFX's inputs) assume an opaque G-buffer; transparent surfaces get forward reflections. | Transparent objects blend into scene colour (the `output` slot) but overwrite normal, roughness, albedo and motion with no blending. | three draws transparent objects in the same pass; splitting them out is engine work for later. | A demo scene with glass (CarConcept windows, ToyCar) shows wrong reflections on or behind transparent surfaces. | open |
| D405 | frame / jitter | FFX and denoiser-core want unjittered projection and the jitter passed separately; three's `velocity` includes jitter unless `velocity.setProjectionMatrix(unjittered)` is set. | `FrameGBuffer.render(scene, camera, { jitter, projection })`: `projection` (unjittered) becomes the contract's `camera.projection` and the history, and drives motion (both frames' clip positions use it), so motion carries no jitter; `camera.projectionJittered` is the rasterised `camera.projectionMatrix`. | D600 | SSSR / confidence output | FFX computes the hit confidence inside intersect and only uses it to lerp radiance to the environment; no confidence target. | Core writes it to an r32float `outputs.confidence` (classify clears to 0, intersect writes traced pixels and their quad copies). At 3840x2160 all-mirror: 7.56M rays, 118310 tiles; 1-D gave 0.0% mirror hits, 2-D 82.1% (`pnpm check`, fail-before shown). External consumers of `indirectArgs` @12 (the denoiser) must index the same way. | Never, unless WebGPU raises the limit. | measured |

| D603 | composite / specular swap | SPEC section 5 and FFX's resolve composite the reflection into a lighting pass that has no specular IBL yet (`radiance x (F0 A + B)` added once). | three's lit image already has its specular IBL, so `screenSpaceComposite` subtracts it and adds the new term: `final = lit + w (ssr - confidence x s x probeEnv)`, probeEnv along three's roughness^4-bent reflection. **W27:** `w` and `s` are three's own values, written by `FrameGBuffer` in the same MRT render from its shader properties (`specular` target, rgba16float: rgb = `specularColorBlended x DFG.x + specularF90 x DFG.y` at three's `roughness`, a = Lagarde occlusion from three's `ambientOcclusion` x `materialEnvIntensity`; `shadingRoughness` r16float for the lookup, D403). So specularIntensity, specularColor, ior (F0 and F90), the AO map's specular occlusion (also `aoNode` / a context `getAO`), envMapIntensity / scene.environmentIntensity and geometry roughness are modelled exactly. Measured (examples/regressions `specularSwap`, 8-bit sRGB mean / p99 at c = 0.5, black hit, vs three's term from its own lighting model): every term 0.03-0.04 / 0.06-0.11 (before: control 0.50 / 13.1, specularIntensity 2.68 / 27.5, specularColor 1.57 / 12.3, ior 1.45 / 6.4, envMapIntensity 1.83 / 15.8, aoMap 2.45 / 38.2, metal 0.74 / 9.3); c = 0 exact. Showcase in-scope px 0.033-0.035 / 0.08 (before 0.66-1.5 / 12-26). Without the target (device under 46 B/sample, `specular: false`) the old estimate: F0 = mix(0.04, base, metalness), f90 1, G-buffer roughness max 0.045, `environmentIntensity`. **Still not modelled** (residual where confidence > 0): clearcoat's own layer (its attenuation of the base layer is folded into the target since W41, D3512), sheen's energy compensation, iridescence's Fresnel, anisotropy's bent normal, retroreflection (all need the lighting-model hook, D400), and a per-material `envMap` (weight and intensity are right, but the radiance subtracted is the scene's probes / environment, not that map's). | No specular-free lit render without a lighting-model hook (same reason as D400); one render, one full-screen pass. The weights are three's shader properties, so they follow three's model without re-deriving it per material. | A specular-off lighting hook exists (D400), or a demo material with clearcoat / sheen / its own envMap shows a halo where SSR blends in. The base-layer clearcoat attenuation `1 - clearcoat x F_Schlick(0.04, 1, dot(Ncc, V))` and sheen's `1 - max(sheen) x IBLSheenBRDF` are cheap to fold into the target's rgb if that happens first. | partly resolved (W27) |

The thickness test is FFX's constant 0.015 (D42), which leaves streaks behind objects (mirrorWall: under the sphere and box). | Keep scene colour free of view-dependent terms; denoise through the seam. | Phase 5 with the denoiser and last-frame lit colour (D69). | open (umbrella: its parts are tracked in D69 / D1002 for black mirrors, D49 and the denoise seam for noise, D66 for occlusion; the thickness default is now hybrid, D1001) |

| D900 | RT / traced-miss confidence | FFX's hybrid sample writes the traced result for every `do_hw` pixel (no confidence after RT). The three adapter (`trace/hitShading.ts`) uses it whenever three lights the scene with probes (`scene.environmentNode = reflectionProbes(probes)`, or the trace option `probes`), over the bake's own atlas and a volume table it packs per frame with core's `packProbeTable`; otherwise `environmentHitShading` (32-direction cosine mean, unchanged). Means over a 16x16 CPU downscale, cached per texture. ToyCar reads 4.5x too bright (hitShadingError ratio 4.46): its emissive map (a few small lights) is averaged over the whole body, and its metalness map is reduced to the scalar 1. | open (W97: base colour maps are sampled at the hit's uv since D5540; emissive, roughness and metalness maps are still means) |

Hit shading vs the on-screen colour of the same hits (960x540): W8: env only 0.41-0.51x the reference (mean abs rel 0.57-0.65); + lights + mean maps 1.14-1.18x (0.32-0.39). **W25 re-measure, same rays (pinned frame index), shadows off -> on:** mirrorWall 0.687 -> 0.672x (0.371 -> 0.380), contact 0.857 -> 0.852x (0.391 -> 0.393), moving 0.909 -> 0.903x (0.326 -> 0.323); @pmndrs/bvh the same to 0.02. Of the off-screen hits the shading shades, shadows darken 7.3% / 0.3% / 0.8%, none brighter. GPU ms of 'rt trace' added at 1080p (default reuse): three-mesh-bvh +0.027 / +0.027 / +0.027 / +0.169 (offscreen / mirrorWall / contact / moving), @pmndrs/bvh +0.024 / +0.009 / +0.021 / +0.081; 960x540 <= +0.052 and <= +0.019. **W28: the dark bias was the indirect term, not the lights.** The kit scenes are lit by their probes (`scene.environmentNode`), so three's diffuse IBL carries the bounce from the lit wall and floor; the hit shading used the sky's mean. With `?probes=0` (three lights from the PMREM'd sky too) the old shading read 0.987 / 1.038 (mirrorWall / contact), so the environment mean vs PMREM and the light terms were never it. With probes at the hit (D901), shadows on: three-mesh-bvh 0.999 / 1.073 / 0.963 (mean abs rel 0.342 / 0.391 / 0.318), @pmndrs/bvh 0.996 / 1.083 / 0.959. The W8 premise (hits ~15% too bright from missing shadows) no longer holds on today's kit scenes: since W12's daylight sky (intensity 1, D1502) the hit shading reads 0.67-0.91x, too dark, and shadows move it by <= 0.02. Kept on by default: it is right where it matters (a hit facing a light behind a blocker), costs <= 0.03 ms on 3 of 4 scenes and the visible error on these scenes is elsewhere. | A scene whose shaded hits sit in cast shadows (interiors, Sponza), to show what shadows buy; per-instance shadow casters (D2600). | measured |

| D1800 | babylon / handedness | three (the contract's first engine) is right-handed: view space looks down −Z, and the tracer assumes it. | Babylon's default world is left-handed (+Z forward in view space). The adapter hands the tracer `V' = Sz·V`, `P' = P·Sz` (`Sz = diag(1, 1, −1, 1)`): clip space unchanged, world space (normals, positions, probe volumes) left as Babylon's. `packages/babylon/src/conventions.ts`. | The frame contract has no handedness flag; converting the matrices is exact and costs nothing. | Core grows a `viewForward` convention, or a second engine needs the same conversion (then it belongs in core). | open |
| D1801 | babylon / texture orientation | The tracer maps texel row 0 to the top of clip space (NDC y = +1), as three's targets are laid out. | Babylon's WebGPU engine draws every render target Y-flipped (row 0 = NDC y −1). The adapter flips the projection it hands the tracer (`Fy·P'`, `Fy = diag(1, −1, 1, 1)`) instead of the inputs, so every core output keeps Babylon's orientation and Babylon samples it upright. `FrameCamera.projection` is therefore the flipped matrix (consistent with the texel rows, which is what the denoise seam needs). | No contract flag for a bottom-left texture origin; flipping the matrix is free, flipping five inputs is not. | A `textureOriginBottom` (or `uvYUp`) convention in core, which pixi or raw WebGL-style engines may also want. | open |
| D1802 | babylon / roughness encoding | The contract read roughness from a channel as is (perceptual or alpha). | Babylon's `PREPASS_REFLECTIVITY.a` is glossiness (`microSurface` = 1 − perceptual roughness). Until W34 one compute dispatch per frame wrote an rgba16float material texture (r = 1 − gloss). | No `roughnessUnpack` mul/add in the contract (normals have one). | — | resolved (W34): `FrameConventions.roughnessUnpack?: { mul, add }` (default identity; roughness = texel × mul + add, before `roughnessIsPerceptual` squares it), applied where core reads contract roughness: the SSSR classify (`loadRoughness`, so `extractedRoughness` and everything downstream of it, the traced pass included, see it already unpacked) and the denoise seam's guides pass. Babylon passes the reflectivity texture's alpha with `BABYLON_ROUGHNESS_UNPACK` { −1, 1 }; the per-frame pass is gone and metalness 1 (D1803) is an r8unorm texture cleared to 1 on resize. Babylon output vs before (`pnpm check --only babylon`, 8-bit screenshots): lit / normal / roughness / SSR-off / probes-off / IBL views max 0 / 255; SSR views differ in 89-286 px, the same as two runs of the old code against each other (88-297 px; the tracer is not bit-deterministic run to run); ray counts, mirror-floor confidence and the IBL numbers unchanged. The only systematic change: roughness is no longer rounded to half float (perceptual ≤ 2.4e-4, alpha ≤ 4.6e-4). three, pixi (identity): `sssr-core` parity bit for bit, `ssr` / `hybrid` / `pixi3d` numbers unchanged. **Follow-up for the lead:** the trace pass (`packages/core/src/trace/`) reads the tracer's `extractedRoughness` (already unpacked), not contract roughness, so it needed no change; if a trace-side read of contract roughness is added, it must apply the unpack too |
| D1803 | babylon / F0 | The contract carries albedo + metalness; F0 = mix(0.04, albedo, metalness). | Babylon's reflectivity target holds F0 (metalness already applied) and no diffuse albedo. The adapter passes albedo = F0 and metalness = 1 (a constant r8unorm since W34; until then from the gloss pass of D1802), so the contract's F0 is exact; any consumer wanting the diffuse albedo gets F0. | The contract can't take a specular-workflow (F0) G-buffer. | A core consumer of diffuse albedo appears (hit shading from the G-buffer, the denoiser's demodulation), or core takes an `f0` input. | open |
| D1804 | babylon / composite | three's composite swaps three's own specular IBL for ours by subtracting an estimate of it (D603); three PBR applies specular AO and multi-scatter energy compensation to that term. | Babylon's PBR renders without its specular IBL (a material plugin zeroes `finalRadianceScaled`), and the composite adds `mix(brdf.xxx, brdf.yyy, F0) × radiance` with Babylon's own BRDF LUT (height-correlated form). No subtraction. **W29:** the terms Babylon puts on that IBL term are applied to ours too. The plugin computes, in Babylon's material shader and under Babylon's own defines, the scalar factor: specular AO `seo` (`environmentRadianceOcclusion`, from the ambient texture) unless BENTNORMAL, horizon occlusion `eho` (`environmentHorizonOcclusion`, with BUMP), bent-normal occlusion, the clear coat's `conservationFactor`, sheen's `sheenAlbedoScaling` (@babylonjs/core 9.35.0 `ShadersWGSL/ShadersInclude/pbrBlockGeometryInfo.js` 12-23, `pbrBlockReflectance.js` 11-17 and 24-28, `pbrBlockFinalLitComponents.js` 63-78, `pbrIBLFunctions.js` 9 and 20). It writes that factor and a multi-scatter flag (`ENVIRONMENTBRDF && MS_BRDF_ENERGY_CONSERVATION`) into the G-buffer's linear velocity z, which Babylon leaves at 0: `(1 − factor) + 2·ms`. The composite multiplies the weight by the factor and, where the flag is set, by Babylon's `getEnergyConservationFactor` `1 + F0 (1 / brdf.y − 1)` (`pbrBRDFFunctions.js` 15), rebuilt from the G-buffer F0 and its own LUT sample. A material without the plugin writes 0 (factor 1, no compensation), so its output is unchanged; so is one with AO and multi-scatter off. `composite.specularFactors = false` restores the bare weight. Still not modelled: D3001 (radiance mixed toward irradiance), D3002 (clear coat by luminance); clear coat and sheen keep Babylon's environment radiance for their own lobes. | Stripping is exact for the base layer and avoids D603's estimate. A spare channel of a target the adapter already reads costs no new MRT target; the material shader already has every term. | Babylon writes something in velocity.z (then a target of its own, or `PREPASS_REFLECTIVITY` alpha if gloss moves), or core takes a per-pixel specular-factor input (see W29's row). | measured (W29, `pnpm check --only babylon`, `?scene=ibl`, SSR and probes off so core's radiance is the environment slot, vs Babylon rendering its own specular IBL, shown 8-bit image, max-channel abs diff / 255): flat sky, all geometry (290776 px) mean 5.19 p99 51 before, 0.11 p99 1 after; pixels with factor < 1 (AO, horizon, bent normal, coat, sheen; 189060 px) 7.43 / 61 → 0.09 / 1; energy compensation only (101716 px) 1.03 / 16 → 0.15 / 1. Gradient sky 2.77 / 28 → 0.47 / 4 (the rest is D3001 and the two prefilters) |
| D1805 | babylon / capture orientation | Core's ProbeSet expects WebGPU's cube convention, with `mirrorX` for three's x-mirrored CubeCamera captures. | A Babylon `ReflectionProbe` cube on WebGPU holds the world mirrored in Y under a plain `texture_cube` lookup (Y-flipped targets plus its swapped ±Y face cameras). The adapter undoes it in one compute pass per capture (swap ±Y, flip each face's rows) before `ProbeSet.capture()`. Measured by the example's orientation check: 16 / 24 directions wrong without it, 24 / 24 with it. **W35:** core now has the general form, `captureTransform` (`packages/core/src/probes/capture.ts`) on ProbeSet, the lookup and ProbeIrradiance: `axes` (a signed axis permutation applied to every world direction at lookup, free; `mirrorX` is the alias `CAPTURE_MIRROR_X`; Babylon's cube as it is: `CAPTURE_MIRROR_Y`) and `faces` (per face a source layer and x / y flips, one remap pass in `ProbeSet.capture()`; Babylon's pass = `CAPTURE_BABYLON_FACES`, pixi-3d's camera images = `CAPTURE_CAMERA_FACES`). The adapter keeps its own pass for now. | Core had no Y mirror; a pass at bake time costs nothing per frame. W35 left the adapter: `BabylonCubeCapture` is public and returns the WebGPU-convention cube (the orientation check reads it), and W34 was editing packages/babylon. | Moving BabylonProbeSet onto `CAPTURE_BABYLON_FACES` (hand core Babylon's raw cube; the remap reads it as a 2D array, as the pass does) or `CAPTURE_MIRROR_Y` with a plain copy (needs COPY_SRC on Babylon's cube). | measured (W35, probe-core, captures made in each convention vs the plain set over 4096 lookups: Babylon faces max 0; Babylon axes float max 3.9e-3, 0 / 255, L1 SH rel 1.1e-6; three axes max 0; pixi-3d camera faces max 0; mirrorX vs CAPTURE_MIRROR_X max 0. Orientation babylon 24 / 24, pixi3d 24 / 24) |
| D1806 | babylon / glossy misses | three's composite looks the probes up at the pixel's own roughness for the part SSR missed (`(1 − confidence) × probe(r)`). | The Babylon composite uses core's radiance as is: FFX SSSR's `lerp(fallback, hit, confidence)`, where a miss's fallback is sampled at roughness 0 (FFX) and only pixels above the roughness threshold take it at their own roughness. Glossy misses are sharper than three's. | Keeps the composite free of probe maths (rule 1) with the core outputs as they are. | Core offers the fallback at the pixel's roughness for misses (an option), or a separate fallback output. | resolved (W30, D3100: core's `fallbackRoughness` defaults to 'surface', so the Babylon and pixi composites get the per-pixel lookup with no adapter change; Babylon mirrorWall sphere misses 17.1 / 89.8 -> 0 / 0 mean / p99 per 255 from the lookup at r) |
| D1807 | babylon / environment intensity | three scales local probes and the environment alike by `scene.environmentIntensity` (the composite's `probeSpecular × intensity`). | The Babylon example draws the sky at `environmentIntensity` (texture level), so the environment slot captured from it already holds env × intensity; the tracer's `iblFactor` stays 1 and local probe captures (real radiance) are not scaled. | A capture of the lit scene should not be dimmed by the environment's intensity. | Matching three pixel for pixel matters, or a scene has a bright local probe next to a dim environment. | open. **W29:** the example drew its sky ~4× too dark: Babylon decodes a gamma-space (default) cube with pow 2.2, and StandardMaterial applies `level` before that decode, so the sky was (0.3 c)^2.2 while PBR lit with 0.3 c^2.2. The cubes are now linear (`gammaSpace = false`) and the sky's level is `intensity^(1/2.2)` (StandardMaterial encodes a linear texture with 1/2.2 and decodes its output with 2.2). Checked: environment-slot radiance = Babylon's own environment radiance to 4 decimals on a flat sky. The environment also gets a GGX prefilter (`HDRFiltering`) and its spherical harmonics (deep imports skip the side effect that computes them; the IBL diffuse was 0) |
| D1700 | RT / @pmndrs/bvh hit normal | Hit shaders use the interpolated shading normal (vertex normals, normal maps). | `Hit.normal` from the @pmndrs/bvh walk is the world **geometric** normal of the authored front side (object-space edge cross product through the instance's inverse transpose; `giTraceClosest` normalizes once; on bvh `main`, W48, `bvhTraceClosest` works it out once after the walk instead of at every accepted triangle). Its GPU layout stores corners only, no vertex normals. **W28:** `Hit.normal` stays geometric (the oracles read it), and the seam gains `capabilities.shadingNormal` + `fn traceHitShadingNormal(hit) -> vec3f`: the caller's vertex normals at `Hit.triangle` (GiHit's input triangle id) weighted by `Hit.barycentrics`, through the instance's normal matrix. @pmndrs/bvh: `BvhMeshGeometry.normals`, packed with the materials, instance normal matrices and triangle corners into the provider's one 'shading' buffer (still 4 storage buffers). three-mesh-bvh port: `normal` in its interleaved attributes (32 B per vertex instead of 16). The pass turns it to the side of the ray-facing geometric normal (D2901). | Agrees with three's `face.normal` through the normal matrix on 100% of oracle hits (camera rays); costs one cross product per accepted triangle. W28: shading normal vs three's interpolated `intersection.normal` on contact, both providers: 3989/3989 camera and 1623/1623 scene hits within cos 0.999 (mean 1e-6 deg); the flat normal was 0.513 / 0.043 deg off on average (toyCar 0.497, carConcept 0.242). Visible on contact's 6-sided cone in the floor (faceted -> smooth, as three shades it); the kit scenes' hit-shading numbers move < 0.01 (dense meshes). GPU ms at 1080p: three-mesh-bvh up to +0.07 (contact; the wider attribute stride slows the walk), @pmndrs/bvh within noise. | Normal maps at the hit (needs UVs and tangents, with D902's per-texel material). | resolved (W28) |
| D1701 | RT / @pmndrs/bvh material id | A per-triangle (or per-geometry-group) material id, as in three's `geometry.groups`, DXR's geometry index, or a hit-group table. | One material index per **instance**, stored as `material + 1` in the upper 24 bits of the instance record's `flags` word (0 = none). `traceHitMaterial` reads two vec4f (albedo, emissive) per material from a provider-owned 4th binding. **W48 (bvh `main`, layout 2):** bits 16-31 of the instance flags (bits 8-15 are now the instance mask), so a material is 0-65534 (`PMNDRS_BVH_MAX_MATERIAL`; the provider throws above 65535 materials). **W49 (bvh `main` be18789, per-triangle materials):** `BvhMeshGeometry.triangleMaterials` (one per triangle, 0-65534 or `PMNDRS_BVH_TRIANGLE_MATERIAL_NONE`) goes to bvh's `MeshInput.triangleMaterials`; every builder writes it into the triangle records' flags bits 16-31, and a hit takes the triangle's material, else the instance's, else none. The provider checks each against its material table. Same WGSL, same 4 storage buffers. | Free: no extra binding in the walk and no extra load (the walk reads that word already); old records read as "no material". | A scene needs per-face materials. **Fired (W49), converged.** | resolved (W49) |
| D1702 | RT / three → @pmndrs/bvh materials | (as D902) | One entry per three `Material` (first material of a multi-material mesh), coloured by `defaultHitMaterial`; meshes sharing a material share an entry; a custom `material` function makes it per mesh. **W49:** a multi-material mesh (material array + `geometry.groups`) stays one mesh tree and one instance, as @pmndrs/bvh's own `/three` folds it; every material its groups draw with gets an entry (`defaultHitMaterial(mesh, { material, materialIndex })`; a custom `material(mesh, source)` is asked once per mesh and material), and its triangles carry their group's (`triangleMaterials`); the instance carries the first material, for triangles no group draws (D3600). A tree is keyed by geometry and group table: two meshes sharing a geometry with different arrays get two trees; an array whose groups all resolve to the instance's entry adds none (same tree as before). `bvhTraceSceneFromObjects` returns `materialSources` and `materialIdOf(mesh, face.materialIndex)`. Check (`--only rt-materials`, multiMaterial scene: one box geometry, groups red / green emissive, arrays swapped on two boxes): before, every hit took its mesh's first material (green 249/321, red 193/214 camera rays); after, 321/321 and 215/215 (scene rays 71/71, 64/64), gpu-hploc and tinybvh alike; id oracle 2552/2552 triangle, instance, material. | Same colours as the three-mesh-bvh stopgap (D902), so hit shading is comparable between the two. | D1701's per-triangle ids land. **Fired (W49), converged.** | resolved (W49) |

| D2000 | pixi / G-buffer source | three and Babylon render the frame contract's targets with their own renderers (MRT G-buffer, geometry renderer task); W15's plan for pixi was the pixi-3d beta or an adapter-owned pass. | pixi.js v8 is 2-D (no depth, normals, roughness or motion), and `@pixi/3d` is a closed beta that must not be bundled or copied into public repos and exposes no normal / roughness targets (its motion vectors and depth output exist only on the maintainer's branch). So the adapter owns a minimal 3-D layer (`ReflectionScene` / `Mesh` / `Material` / `Camera`, `Geometry` builders) and one MRT pass on pixi's device, in the contract's native conventions (no D1800-D1803 conversions). Its lighting is the adapter's own small PBR: one sun (GGX + Lambert, 3x3 PCF shadow map), hemisphere diffuse ambient, emissive; no punctual lights beyond the sun, no IBL diffuse, no transparency. `packages/pixi/src/{scene,gbuffer}.ts`. | It is the only way a pixi user gets real reflections today, and owning the pass means the conventions match the contract exactly. | `@pixi/3d` ships publicly with depth, normal, roughness / metalness and velocity outputs (its issues #28 / #29): a `fromPixi3D` frame builder replaces the adapter's layer for its meshes. | superseded by D2100 (W19) |
| D2001 | pixi / 2-D content | (no source: three / Babylon content is 3-D already) | pixi content enters the 3-D layer only as textures: a material's `map` / `emissiveMap` / `roughnessMap` are pixi `Texture`s (typically `RenderTexture`s of Text, Graphics, Sprites, rendered before `view.render()`). The adapter samples the whole source (a sub-texture's frame or an atlas region is ignored), decodes base and emissive maps as sRGB, reads the roughness map linear, and treats emissive as premultiplied (transparent = black). pixi display objects are never geometry. | Keeps the bridge to one public pixi call (`renderer.texture.getGpuSource`). | Atlased sprite textures are wanted on meshes (pass the frame as a uv transform), or someone wants pixi meshes lifted into 3-D. | superseded by D2100 (W19: pixi content reaches 3-D through pixi-3d's `ContainerTexture` / any pixi Texture on a Material3D) |
| D2002 | pixi / output textures | three and Babylon wrap core's output GPUTextures as engine textures without copies (Babylon: `wrapWebGPUTexture`). | pixi has no public way to wrap a foreign GPUTexture in a `TextureSource`, and its WGSL layouts bind every `texture_2d<f32>` as sampleType `float`, which r32float (confidence, hit distance) can't satisfy. One compute pass per frame repacks core's outputs into two rgba16float pixi storage textures (radiance + confidence; hit distance, ray length, alpha roughness). | Public pixi API only; the pass is a few hundredths of a ms at 960x540 (not timed separately). | Core takes an output allocator (like `@pmndrs/upscaler`'s `TextureAllocator`), so the adapter allocates pixi-owned outputs and the confidence lands in a format pixi binds. | open |
| D2003 | pixi / device features | three's adapter requests `subgroups` and core's limits through `WebGPURenderer({ requiredFeatures, requiredLimits })`; Babylon's `enableAllFeatures`. | pixi's own `requestDevice` asks for neither, so on pixi's default device the tracer runs its workgroup variant with four storage textures per stage. `requestReflectionsDevice()` builds an adapter + device with pixi's optional features plus `subgroups`, `timestamp-query` and `reflectionsRequiredLimits(adapter)`, for `app.init({ gpu })`. | pixi has an option to take an external device; that is the public route. | pixi adds `requiredFeatures` / `requiredLimits` to its init options. | open |
| D2004 | pixi / split-sum term | three's composite uses three's DFG LUT (r187 `DFGLUT`: 16 x 16 rg16f, u = perceptual roughness, v = N.V at texel centres, single-scattering GGX with **height-correlated** Smith visibility; W34 reproduced it by integration to max 0.015, mean 0.0005, the rest is its 4096-sample noise at grazing N.V; multi-scatter is a separate term in three, not in the table), Babylon's its environment BRDF LUT (height-correlated); pixi-3d's own `generateBrdfLut` integrates the **separable** UE4 form (Schlick-GGX, k = alpha / 2; 256 x 256, 1024 samples). | Until W34 the pixi composite filter used Karis' analytic `EnvBRDFApprox` (2014), core had no DFG term, and pixi-3d's LUT is out of a Filter's reach. **W34:** core exports the term (`packages/core/src/brdf/splitSum.ts`): `splitSumDFGWgsl(visibility)` (WGSL `fn splitSumDFG(NdotV, roughness) -> vec2f`, bilinear over a 32 x 32 `const` table, no bindings), `SPLIT_SUM_DFG_WGSL` (height-correlated), `SPLIT_SUM_DFG_TABLES`, and the integrator `integrateSplitSumDFG` the tables came from (65536 Hammersley samples per texel). The pixi `ReflectionsFilter` uses `splitSumDFGWgsl('separable')`, pixi-3d's own form, so the term it adds back is the one REFLECTIONS_SCENE_COLOR_BIT subtracted with. | Matching the engine's own LUT, as the three and Babylon composites do; the maintainer kept separable for pixi (2026-11-11, Q8). 32 x 32 rather than three's 16 x 16: bilinear error vs a 64 x 64 reference drops ~3x (F0 = 1 weight: HC mean 0.0022 → 0.0007, max 0.074 → 0.047; separable max 0.19 → 0.11, all in the grazing, low-roughness corner a glossy floor sits in). A WGSL const table, not a texture: no binding to thread through pixi (D2002), ~16 KB of shader source per form. | pixi-3d exposes its BRDF LUT to filters (then bind it); or pixi-3d switches its LUT form (then switch the filter's `visibility`). | measured (W34; weight = F0 x A + B over a 64 x 64 (N.V, roughness) grid, abs diff): core HC vs three's DFGLUT: F0 0.04 mean 0.0007 p99 0.011, F0 1 mean 0.0019 p99 0.031 max 0.049 (16 x 16 three vs the reference: 0.0024 / 0.044 / 0.075). EnvBRDFApprox vs three: F0 0.04 mean 0.014 p99 0.072 max 0.128; F0 1 mean 0.11 p99 0.37 max 0.44 (Karis' fit runs ~0.05-0.15 low across mid roughness, and is a fit of the separable form: vs separable reference F0 1 mean 0.061 p99 0.19). Separable vs height-correlated (the form choice itself): F0 1 mean 0.083 p99 0.25. **pixi composite change** (`pnpm check --only pixi3d`, Karis → core separable, 8-bit screenshots, max-channel abs /255, geometry px): SSR and probes off (deterministic) mean 0.63 p99 3 max 7; SSR off with probes mean 1.67 p99 10 max 25; final (SSR + probes + denoiser) mean 1.96 p99 11, reflections mostly brighter (signed mean +0.59); run-to-run noise of the final image alone mean 0.30 p99 5. Not measured: the env-only composite against pixi-3d rendering its own IBL (Babylon's `iblCompare` analogue; Fine-tune backlog) |
| D2005 | pixi / composite mapping | three and Babylon composite in a full-screen pass over the G-buffer. | The composite is a pixi `Filter` on the view sprite, and it reads the G-buffer by the position inside the filter's bounds (the 0..1 vertex attribute pixi feeds every filter), not through its input texture: pixi renders a filter's input into an 8-bit texture, which would clip the HDR colour. So the filtered object must be an unrotated rectangle showing the whole view (scaling and translation are fine; rotation, skew or a partial crop are not). The filter is also the present step: exposure, ACES fit, sRGB encode for pixi's 8-bit canvas. | A pixi-native composite that users can stack with other filters, with HDR intact. | pixi filters can take an HDR input format, or someone wants the view rotated (then sample the input texture after a float-format pre-pass). | open |
| D2006 | pixi / submits | three and Babylon record core's passes into the engine's command flow (Babylon flushes, then one submit). | The view records G-buffer + tracer in one encoder and submits it; with a denoiser, core's seam submits its own guides pass and the denoiser its own work, and the repack is a third submit. pixi then renders the stage (its own submit). | The denoise seam's `dispatch` owns its submission (and so does `FfxReflectionDenoiser`). | Core's seam (and denoiser-core) takes an encoder, so an adapter can keep one submit per frame. | superseded by the second D2006 row below (same issue, rewritten for pixi-3d in W19) |
| D2400 | hybrid / blend with the probe (W22) | FFX SSSR and its hybrid sample: radiance = `lerp(fallback, hit, confidence)`, one confidence (screen-edge vignette x squared thickness falloff). Neither asks whether the fallback can be what the ray sees. | three composite, `depthAware` 1 by default: the vignette v is recomputed at the hit (D2401) and split off from c; of the thickness part's remainder (1 - c/v), the share of the probe term whose box-projected surface lies behind the hit goes to the hit instead: w = max(c, v (1 - (1 - c/v) k)), k = core `probeProxyShare` (the lookup's volume weights; a volume counts if its projection-box distance along R is < 1.1 h, fading to 0 at 1.25 h, h = hit distance; the environment and unprojected volumes are at infinity). Rejected rays (c = 0) are unchanged. The signal the denoiser takes uses w, so the denoised path follows. `depthAware: 0` (examples/ssr `?depthAware=0`, the checkbox) is FFX's blend. | A box-projected probe shows the room's walls, never the object in front of them, so a sphere hit with thickness-partial confidence let the wall's cells show through the reflected sphere (examples/ssr mirrorWall `lowSphere`). Measured there (960x540, denoised): mean probe weight on the 41464 floor px whose exact reflection hits the sphere 0.048 -> 0.004. Cost: the 17669 px whose reflection passes behind the sphere to the wall (the probe was right) take hit weight 0.009 -> 0.095 (pose `low` 0.058 -> 0.15, `default` 0.06 -> 0.11). Other kit scenes: < 1.2% of px change by > 8/255 (explainer, the most). | D2401 | hybrid composite / screen-edge fade (W22) | FFX folds the vignette into `confidence` inside the intersect pass. | The composite recomputes FFX's vignette (`smoothstep` over a 0.05 x (h/w, 1) border) at P + reflect(-V, N) x hitDistance, projected by the traced frame's view-projection (`ScreenSpaceReflectionsPass.uniforms.viewProjection`, new); c / v is then the thickness part. Exact for mirrors. For glossy rays the sampled direction differs, so the estimate is approximate near the border; the weight never drops below c. | No new tracer output: core's confidence keeps its meaning for the miss list, D900 and the debug views, and W21 is changing the denoiser wiring in the same files. | Seams at the screen border on glossy surfaces, or the tracer gets a second channel (fade, validity). | open |
| D2402 | hybrid / thickness-rejected hits on hidden faces (W22) | No source fixes this: one depth layer can't tell "inside the object" from "behind it". | A ray that ends behind a surface by more than the thickness (c = 0) still takes the probe, even where the probe's surface lies behind that surface. Turning those into hits would make every ray that passes behind an object streak: on `lowSphere`, 87.6% of the px that reflect the wall behind the sphere are c = 0, against 0.4% of the sphere's own. Two things remain on `lowSphere`: a thin crescent near the contact, and the probe's own capture of the sphere, box-projected onto the wall (the grey disc around the reflection, the "probe ghost"). | `?rt=1` answers both: sphere probe weight 0, and the behind px are traced to the wall. A back-face (min/max) depth pass (McGuire and Mara 2014) would separate the two cases without rays. | open (W37: the kit scenes keep their free-standing objects out of the captures, D4301, which removes the probe ghost where it showed; the thickness streak half stays, D4303) |

| D2100 | pixi / G-buffer source (W19, supersedes D2000, D2001) | three and Babylon render the frame contract's targets with their own renderers (MRT G-buffer, geometry renderer task). | The scene is pixi-3d's (`@pixi/3d`, closed beta): a View3D rendered into an rgba16float RenderTexture is the scene colour, `DepthOutputPass` the depth, `MotionVectorPass` the motion, `Camera3D` the matrices. The adapter never imports the beta: it takes pixi Textures and structural shapes (`packages/pixi/src/pixi3d.ts`), and the shader data it hands pixi-3d is plain objects. The one class that needs the beta's runtime (`ReflectionsGBufferPass`, a `Pass3D`) lives in `examples/pixi3d/shared/` with the probe-capture cameras; the beta resolves only from the gitignored `examples/pixi3d/vendor/` (the upscaler's split). | The beta must not be committed or bundled, and a homemade 3-D layer isn't something pixi users adopt. | `@pixi/3d` goes public: the shared classes move into the adapter (or into pixi-3d, `docs/research/pixi3d-needs.md`). | open |
| D2101 | pixi-3d / material values | Engines write normal, roughness, metalness and albedo from the material's own shading (textures, normal maps). | pixi-3d has no G-buffer output, and a custom pass shader can't read the material struct or textures (its compile path skips the material plugins). `REFLECTIONS_GBUFFER_SHADER` redraws the opted-in opaque meshes once (a `ForwardRenderPass` on its own membership, like `MotionVectorPass`) and reads two texels of each material's row in pixi-3d's store directly: texel 0 `baseColor`, texel 1 `pbrParams` (Material3D's packing puts pbrMaterialBit's vec4s first; 0.1.0-beta.2 layout: 16 texels per material, 4096-wide rows). Normals are the interpolated vertex normals (front-facing). Packed into one rgba32float (oct normal, roughness 10 bit + metalness 8 bit, base colour 3 x 8 bit as exact integers), unpacked by the adapter. So base-colour, metallic-roughness and normal maps, flat shading and pixi-3d's geometric specular AA never reach the reflections; roughness is the material constant. | The closest input without rebuilding a renderer: one geometry-only pass, live material values (a material edit shows the next frame). GPU-checked: mirror and gold-torus values exact, G-buffer normals vs depth-derived normals 0.9976 mean dot. | pixi-3d exposes a G-buffer / normal + material output pass, or lets a pass shader reuse the material's bits with its own template (MRT). | open |

The multiple-scattering compensation (`FmsEms`) stays in, and so do clearcoat / sheen environment lobes. Needs a view `environment` (iblBit and its BRDF table); materials without the bit (e.g. a glTF's) keep pixi-3d's specular and get the reflection on top. | pixi-3d's only hook into the material shader is construction-time `extraBits`; the term is cheap to recompute.
