| D3100 | SSSR / miss fallback roughness (W30) | FFX SSSR intersect: `radiance = lerp(env(sampled direction, roughness 0), hit, confidence)`; the sharp environment sample is left for the denoiser to blur. Only pixels above the roughness threshold sample the environment at their own roughness (mirror direction). | `settings.fallbackRoughness`, default `'surface'`: every stored pixel (the ray's and its quad copies) blends to the rough-pixel lookup of its own: mirror direction, `sqrt(alpha)` (perceptual at the seam, D43), the same mix by confidence. Mirror pixels (alpha < 1e-4) keep FFX's lookup, so mirrors, confidence-1 pixels and a constant fallback (three's) are bit for bit unchanged. `'mirror'` is FFX's. | Composites that show core radiance as is (Babylon, pixi) showed a mirror-sharp environment on glossy floors (D1806). Measured as `|radiance - lookup(r)|` per channel, 0-255 after c/(1+c)^(1/2.2), 960x540: sssr-core ?env 14678 glossy-miss px mirror 3.54 / p99 85.0 -> 0 / 0; Babylon mirrorWall sphere (alpha 0.12) 2115 px 17.1 / 89.8 -> 0 / 0, 1971 partial-confidence px 1.37 / 10.2 -> 0.004 / 0.02; pixi3d arcade floor 186532 px 33.6 / 118 -> 0 / 0. Through FFX's denoiser (pixi3d, 189526 miss px, 32 frames): mirror 23.7 / 85.3, surface 3.0 / 39.5, so the denoiser does not close the gap. 0 mirror / confidence-1 px change on any page. Intersect at 1080p +0.01 ms (0.41 -> 0.42). | A denoiser that needs the unfiltered sample (pass 'mirror'), or the trace pass: traced misses still look up at roughness 0 (D900, `core/src/trace/`), so a hybrid frame mixes the two on its RT pixels. | measured |
| D3200 | probes / bounces | C (interior branch): `bake({ bounces, usePrevious })` re-captures with the previous pass's probes visible, through r187-dev lighting hooks (`indirectSpecular()` / `indirectDiffuse()`). | `bake({ bounces: n })`, default 1: passes 2..n re-capture the captured probes with lookups on (`capturing` stays false), every capture sampling the previous pass's atlas (Jacobi: each pass prefilters into a scratch copy and copies its slots in at the end, so the result doesn't depend on probe order). No `usePrevious`. | Our node is the materials' whole IBL (`scene.environmentNode`), so captures need no lighting hooks: a captured diffuse wall gets the previous pass as irradiance, a glossy one as reflection. Rooms, 256 px: bake 3 probes 4.0 / 8.6 / 13.5 ms at 1 / 2 / 3 bounces; each extra bounce changes the image ~0.27x the previous (2: p99 42-49, 3: p99 9-12, 4: p99 3). | Bounces should be the default (D3201: it removes most of mirrorWall's SSR-to-probe level bias), or a live bake needs them cheaper (bounce only some probes, or reuse the last frame's atlas as the previous pass). | measured (W31); default 2 since W37 (D4300) |
| D3201 | probes / capture-time lighting vs view time | idTech 8 / Lagarde: probes are relit or normalized so a reflection matches what the camera sees of the same surface (re-fit, D6). | Captures see local probes off (`capturing`), so a captured surface's indirect light is the environment slot only, while the same surface on screen (an SSR hit's scene colour) gets its indirect diffuse from the probes. The probe then shows that surface darker than SSR does. | Avoids the feedback loop of a probe in its own capture (D25), at the cost of one bounce. | W31 measured it as the main cause of the SSR-to-probe radiance step on mirrorWall (seam metric, ssr example, probes re-baked in-page, 128 px): floor r .3 bias (probe - hit) -18.4 -> +5.2 / 255 luma and step 21.7 -> 7.9 with `bounces: 2` (3: +5.8 / 7.9); r .1 -25.9 -> +9.0, step 36.1 -> 17.1. Ruled out there: direct + IBL specular in the captures (D400; capture materials with `specularIntensity` 0: bias moves 0.3-1.0, step +0.2), resolution (256: step -0.4), the environment intensity (D1502: 1 in these scenes, background = environment), the lod mapping (r187: 0.1). contact is different: bias ~0 (r .2: 0.05; low pose r .3: -2.7) with step 25-33, i.e. the step is spatial, not a level: the probe shows the box (wall, sky) where SSR hits an object standing on the floor (box-projection proxy error for objects off the box, D2400's case); `bounces: 2` there adds a +7..+15 bias. Also seen: re-baking with the probe node installed gives mirrorWall probes ~8 luma darker than the page's first bake (bias -10.6 -> -18.4), whose captures were lit by three's PMREM of the sky instead of our environment slot; not explained (backlog). | `bounces: 2` as a default for static bakes; the PMREM vs environment-slot irradiance gap is explained. | measured (W31); `bounces: 2` is the default since W37 (D4300) |
| D3300 | SSSR / mirror-bias form (W32) | SSSR15 writes the bias as `u.x = lerp(u.x, 1, bias)` on its GGX NDF sampler (its mirror end at u = 1). three's SSRNode (r186, `mirrorBias` default 0.5, the compare page's D2301) samples a bounded-VNDF spherical cap and pulls `Xi.y` toward the cap top by a random amount, `mix(Xi.y, 0, mirrorBias * sqrt(Xi.w))`. | `U1 *= 1 - mirrorBias` on Heitz 2018's VNDF: a fixed truncation of the disk radius, no random amount. At grazing views the disk centre is not the mirror direction (VNDF), so the bias pulls toward a microfacet tilted to the viewer: CPU sweep, alpha 0.2, 85 deg view, mean angle to the mirror 36.5 deg at 0, 25.9 deg at 1 (13.0 deg at 0.5 vs 30.0 at 0 for a normal view). | The same idea on our sampler; three's 0.5 is therefore not the same lobe as our 0.5 (theirs pulls less on average: the mean of `sqrt(Xi.w)` is 2/3). | The compare page needs matched lobes, or grazing glossy floors show the tilt. | open |
| D3500 | examples / temporal AA (W40, briefed as W26) | three r187's `TRAANode` jitters the camera from inside a RenderPipeline (`OnBeforeRenderPipeline`), dilates motion to the closest depth in 3x3, rejects disocclusions against the previous depth, clips with variance (gamma 0.5-1) and blends 5%+ current. The FFX SSSR sample and the denoiser repo's demo run a TAA after the reflections. | `examples/shared/taa.ts`: Halton (2,3) jitter applied before `ssr.render` and passed as `{ jitter, projection }` (D405), so the frame contract's motion and the denoiser are unjittered; motion at the pixel (no dilation, no depth test); variance clip gamma 0.75-1.25 in motion, widening to 3 at rest; 5%+ current in motion, a running average of up to 128 frames at zero motion; Catmull-Rom history; on the tone-mapped display image. examples/ssr: off under `?manual`. | The pages render the scene through FrameGBuffer before the display pipeline, so TRAANode's in-pipeline jitter would jitter the wrong render. At rest a 5% blend with a tight box leaves the jitter visible on edges (car region 0.08 -> 0.57/255 mean |diff|, measured); the running average settles it (0.07). | Ghosting on thin edges over moving backgrounds, or the maintainer wants three's TRAA: render the G-buffer inside a RenderPipeline pass. | superseded (W36): no longer the default; the pages run @pmndrs/upscaler (D4200). Still selectable as `?aa=taa` / the panel's 'TAA (W40)', through `examples/shared/antialias.ts` |

Trace setting `hitSpecular` (default on) turns it off: the ssr-rt D901 checks compare hits with the on-screen colour, which has no specular (hitColor 'current', D400), so they run with it off. `defaultHitMaterial` fills it from the material's scalars x map means (one set per mesh, D902) and drops diffuse by `transmission`. Revisit with per-texel materials (D902) or a cone of visibility rays. | measured |

| D3570 | examples / roughness from normal-map variance, `?specAA` (W46; D3552) | Toksvig 2005: fold the variance a minified normal map loses into the specular lobe from the averaged normal's length; Karis 2013 ("Normalmap filtering", after Han et al. 2007): vMF fit, `kappa = (3r - r^3) / (1 - r^2)`, `alpha'^2 = alpha^2 + 2 / kappa`. Kaplanyan 2016 / Tokuyoshi 2019 add the screen-space normal derivatives instead. three shades the normalized average at the authored roughness. | An examples-side material option (`examples/showcase/src/specAA.ts`, not package API): every standard / physical material with a normal map gets a `roughnessNode` = `(alpha^4 + s^2 x max(0, 2/kappa(r) - 2/kappa(r0)) x k)^(1/4)`, capped at 1: r = length of the raw sample at three's uv and texture matrix (hardware mip), r0 = the same at level 0 (subtracts the 8-bit / bilinear baseline, r0 ~0.996: no change where the map is magnified), s^2 = normalScale^2 (the slope variance scales by it), k = `?specAA=<k>` (default 1, a uniform). three's lit term and the G-buffer read the same node (no package change: `gbufferRoughness` already follows `roughnessNode`, `gbufferShadingRoughness` reads three's `roughness`); the probe bake renders the same materials. Showcase: a "Materials" checkbox and `?specAA=0|1|<k>`; compare page: every raster method (ours, three's SSR, probes), never the path tracer. **Default on** (`SPEC_AA_DEFAULT`). No derivative (geometric) term. | It is what the reference does: the PT supersamples the flake map. G-buffer roughness at the door 0.25 -> 0.35 (shading 0.267 -> 0.367). Cost ~+0.1-0.15 ms of three's render passes at 1280x720 (two extra samples per normal-mapped fragment). | Sponza + RT gets 1.3 MAD worse: the polished floor (r 0.06, scale 0.15) blurs and brightens a little at distance, the same direction as Sponza's existing ~25-45 bias vs the PT in the shade (not this option); revisit once that bias is gone. A user-facing package option would need a three node material hook, not this examples override. 2/kappa underestimates the flake map's exact scaled slope variance a little (0.0117 vs 0.0133 at the full average): k > 1 if a scene needs it. | measured |

On when `ao` is passed. (3) `reflectionProbes(probes, { ao })` / `sampleReflectionProbes(.., { ao, view })`: multiplies the lookup; as `scene.environmentNode` that changes three's `lit` for both radiance and irradiance (r187's EnvironmentNode reads the same node for both; at the irradiance context's level 1 the factor is ~ao). Table: header row 2 = each atlas's maxLod, row 3 = its first irradiance slot, volume row +4.w = its atlas (all zero for one atlas). Lookup: `atlases` / `volumeAtlases`; three unrolls the volumes and names each one's atlas at compile time (no branch); the runtime loop (core ProbeSet) branches on the table's index. | D4950 | probes / which volumes a pixel walks (W63) | #35046: every volume, every px, unrolled into every material. HDRP: env probes in the per-view tile (opaque) / cluster (transparent) light list, built by compute each frame, evaluated while `reflectionHierarchyWeight < 1`. Unreal / Godot Forward+ / id Tech: clustered (froxel) lists per view. The lookup walks the set bits of P's cell in table order and stops once the weight is spent; a volume outside P's cell has no weight at P, so the result is the full walk's. Up to `PROBE_UNROLL_MAX_VOLUMES` (4) known volumes it still unrolls the full walk. | Probes are static (baked), so a camera-independent list needs no per-frame build, works for any P (traced hits, captures, other views) and is the same on every adapter. 32 volumes at 1080p: 9.6 -> ~0.8 ms (`docs/research/probe-lookup-cost.md`). | A scene too large for 4096 cells (cells several metres wide holding many volumes: a two-level grid or more cells), probes that move every frame, or a lookup that wants froxels anyway (lights or decals sharing the list). | measured |

'frame': the corner cycles tl, br, tr, bl with the frame index; 'pattern': a 4x4 Latin square over quads (rows 0123 / 2301 / 1230 / 3012), offset by the frame index. A corner past an odd render size's edge falls back to the top-left. spq 2 flips the diagonal's parity. Copy flags keep naming the base's x / y / diagonal neighbour, so intersect, the RT pass and `quadCopy: 'sameSurface'` (D3740, tested against the chosen base) need no change. `Params.quadBaseRotation` (u32 141). | A fixed copy grid is a 2x2 pattern TAA / the upscaler can't average away (backlog: contact drifts in blocks at rest). sssr-core: subgroup = workgroup lists and images with frame, pattern, pattern spq 2, pattern + sameSurface. carConcept w50 960x540 vs PT (32 spp, MAD): see README; frame / pattern change the frame within 0.1 and the lip with RT 48.8 -> 43.5 / 43.1. | **W71 phase 2: keep as option.** 2x2 energy drops (contact AA off 0.34 -> 0.28, rooms 0.085 -> 0.055, chess 0.071 -> 0.044), rest drift doesn't, orbit flicker rises where AA doesn't hide it (contact AA off 0.27 -> 0.38-0.40, upscale 1.5 8.5 -> 9.2-9.4, carConcept +2-4 %): fails the plan's rule. Contact's 4.5/255 rest flicker under the upscaler is unchanged by any rotation (not the quad copy). D3663 lip (+ RT, hitSource) 32.2 -> 27.8, sameSurface 20.5. classify +0.06 ms at 1080p. Revisit with X3's ray reuse (which removes the copy). | measured; option (off) |

The rest of the test (FFX's smoothstep falloff, squared) is unchanged. `Params.thicknessDepthFactor` (f32 142), bit 1 of `Params.x1Flags` (u32 144). | One number for a toy car and a hall instead of per-scene thicknesses (W11, D4303). Phase 1 sweep (toyCar, carConcept, Sponza): README. | **W71 phase 2: keep as option.** 'add' makes thin geometry worse (lineSpreadThin r 0.05 denoised x4.25 -> x5.51 at 0.02; streaks 4.6 -> 6.5 %). Candidate default for the maintainer: 'relative' 0.02 with RT on. | **W76: the default with RT on** ('relative' 0.02 when `missesTraced`; explicit values win; D5102). RT off unchanged. |

Why removed: the lobe filter only touches the probe / environment lookup of re-shaded and traced hits, a fraction of a mip; the over-blur at grazing is the denoiser's and the cone's. The RT pass's second pipeline is gone; SSSR word 143 is the cone's, RT word 67 padding. |

Why removed: no measurable change, and it acts only where hits read last frame's colour without the re-shade (not the default). Bit 0 of SSSR word 144 stays 0. |

'copy' (default) is FFX bit for bit. Built on the first 'ratio' frame. | A sample that stands for a 2x2 quad shows as a 2x2 grid to TAA and the denoiser (D3663, T2, T10); reuse makes one ray per quad act like several (SSSR 2015: 1 ray + 4 reuse ~ 4 rays). An 8-level rgba16float pyramid (6 at first; 8 since the cap test) of the hit-colour source (this frame's scene colour without specular; last frame's for `hitColor: 'history'` with `hitShading: 'off'`), built each frame by the maintainer's `downsampler` (level 0 a converting copy, then 2x2 averages). At a hit: cone full angle = the reflected GGX lobe's FWHM, 2.574 alpha (half-vector HWHM tan 0.6436 alpha, doubled) x scale x the filter share (D5021); width = 2 rayLength tan(angle / 2), projected at the hit's depth; mip = log2(px), trilinear; below mip 0 the texel as before (contacts, mirrors). With the re-shade it filters the hit's diffuse (screen) part only. No grazing shrink (X1 #3), no depth-aware pyramid, not applied to the RT pass's on-screen reused hits (RT_SOURCE 4). Intersect +1 sampled texture +1 sampler (D4900 count 10 -> 11). Cost at 1080p: pyramid pass +0.24-0.26 ms, intersect within noise. | Raw fireflies from single-texel hits. Raw, 32 frames (default -> cone): mirrorWall r 0.35 sphere relStd 0.79 -> 0.56, p999 23.9 -> 4.0 x mean, mean -3 %; pixi3d arcade floor relStd 0.43 -> 0.31, fireflies 2.7 -> 1.25 %; carConcept paint unchanged (its variance is the re-shaded specular). W45 energy (door / skirt denoiser loss) unchanged. The showcase and its compare page did not use `reflectionsRequiredLimits` and now raise this one limit themselves (the first full `pnpm check` after the switch stopped there: 9 > 8 with probe hit shading). **An app that builds its device without `reflectionsRequiredLimits` and turns RT on with probe hit shading breaks until it raises the limit.** | The scene copy's two-buffer layout is @pmndrs/raytracer's (D4 there); merging it into one would cost every other consumer a rebuild of that layout. | A device with 8 storage buffers needs the probe fallback + probe hit shading together, or the raytracer scene copy becomes one buffer. | resolved (W89, D5450: the scene copy is bound packed, 4 buffers; three's traced pass with probe hit shading binds 8 and runs on a default device; a device that can't fit a configuration degrades, D5451) |
| D5201 | RT / hit shading by @pmndrs/raytracer's `rtShade` (W81, P1.5) | D11 / D901 / D903: emission + albedo x (probe or sky irradiance + shadowed directional / hemisphere lights) + the specular lobe on the probes / sky; emission on either side. A path tracer's first bounce: emission (front side) + direct light from every light (area lamps too) with MIS. | Opt-in `raytracerHitShading(device)` (needs a provider with `capabilities.hitShade`, i.e. the raytracer one): `traceHitShade` = `rtShade` FULL, emission (front side only) + one light sample (power-picked among emissive triangles, ONE sun = three's brightest DirectionalLight, the environment if the app passes one) with its shadow ray, @pmndrs/raytracer's builtin Lambert + GGX BSDF; seeded per pixel (`hsoPixel`) and frame (`setFrame`). **No indirect term**: the probes hold the emitters and the sky too, so adding their irradiance counts those twice. Hemisphere lights, extra directional lights, point / spot lights and three's own `scene.environment` texture are not read. The default stays the probe / environment shading. | Measured against @pmndrs/pathtracer at maxDepth 1 (pathtracer `bench/pages/reflections-ab.ts`, `docs/research/w81/`): per material within 0.3% on diffuse and gold (the old shading: 0.00x on Cornell, 0.12x / 0.92x on a lamp- / sun-lit floor, 0.12x on gold); a saturated plastic reads 1.62x OpenPBR's coated walk (1.11x pure Lambert): the builtin BSDF is three-like, not OpenPBR layered. | The maintainer picks the default hit shading (question in HANDOFF W81); a probe term without the emitters / sky (a "bounce-only" probe) would let rtShade's direct and the probes' indirect add. | measured (W89: the raytracer is the only provider, so this applies to every traced pass, D5452) |
| D5202 | RT / instance moves rebuild the whole scene copy (W81) | The @pmndrs/bvh wrapper rewrote only the instances' normal matrices on `setTransforms`. | `RaytracerTraceProvider.setTransforms` rebuilds the top level (as before) and the whole @pmndrs/raytracer scene copy (instance records, light areas and powers), then writes it into the same buffers when it fits. CPU cost grows with the scene, not with the moved instances. | @pmndrs/raytracer has no incremental scene-copy update yet (its TASKS P1.4b). The GPU trace cost of `moving` is unchanged (0.499 vs 0.493 ms floors). | A scene with many triangles and moving instances (CPU frame time), or P1.4b lands. | open (W89: the raytracer is the only provider, so this applies to every traced pass, D5452) |
| D5203 | RT / @pmndrs/raytracer's module inside our pass (W81) | @pmndrs/raytracer builds its WGSL for the device's caps: `enable f16; enable subgroups;` first when the device has them. | The provider builds caps with no optional features (`buildCaps({ features: new Set(), limits })`), so its module has no `enable` lines (WGSL refuses them mid-file, and the provider's code is pasted after `HIT_WGSL`), and minifies it (`minify: true`, line breaks kept: the module with `rtShade` is ~50 KB unminified). Our WGSL names starting `rt`/`RT_` (`RT_SOURCE`, `rtLobeVisibility`, `rtHitSpecularOn`, ...) share @pmndrs/raytracer's prefix; none collides today (checked against its 169 names). | The inline walk uses no subgroup or f16 code. | @pmndrs/raytracer starts needing an optional feature inline, or a new name of ours (or theirs) collides. | open (W89: the raytracer is the only provider, so this applies to every traced pass, D5452) |
| D5204 | RT / material records through @pmndrs/raytracer (W81) | The @pmndrs/bvh wrapper stored the seam's own fields (albedo, F0, roughness, clear coat, emissive) and returned them as given. | Materials become @pmndrs/raytracer's OpenPBR records (`raytracerMaterialOf`): three's `defaultHitMaterial` now also gives `baseColor`, `metalness`, `transmission` and the record is metal-rough; without them, metalness is solved from `albedo` and `specular` by three's formulas (exact for a grey base). `traceHitMaterial` / `traceHitSpecular` decode the record back: albedo = base (1 - m)(1 - transmission), F0 = mix(0.04 specularWeight, base, m). A non-pbr three material (no `specular`) keeps no specular lobe (specularWeight 0). Colour precision is f32, roughness / weights unorm16. | One material table for the trace core, its shading and the path tracer. | A material whose F0 is not mix(0.04, base, m) (three's `specularIntensity` / `specularColor`) matters on traced hits. | open (W89: the raytracer is the only provider, so this applies to every traced pass, D5452) |
| D5300 | RT / the @pmndrs/raytracer provider stays additive until it is published (W82, T69; D5200-D5204) | W81 (P1.5) replaced core's `@pmndrs/bvh` wrapper (`createPmndrsBvhTraceProvider`) with `createRaytracerTraceProvider` and deleted the wrapper. | Both exist. The `@pmndrs/bvh` wrapper (npm `^0.1.0`) is the shipped default of core, the three adapter, `examples/ssr` and the showcase; `@pmndrs/raytracer` (not on npm, pmndrs/pathtracer is private) is an optional peer that only the opt-in paths import on demand: `createRaytracerTraceProvider[FromObjects]`, `trace.raytracerHitShading` / `pass.setRaytracerHitShading` (T70, D5201), examples `&bvhCore=raytracer&rtShade=1` and the showcase's checkbox / `?rtShade=1`. No devDependency link: `examples/vite.config.ts` resolves it from a sibling `pathtracer/packages/raytracer` (or `$RAYTRACER_REPO`) like the denoiser, else aliases `shared/raytracer-missing.ts` and sets `__RAYTRACER_AVAILABLE__` false (the pages hide the option); core types it with a shorthand `declare module`. D5200-D5204 describe the raytracer provider, i.e. only apply to the opt-in path here; the default keeps the wrapper's 4 storage buffers. | A fresh clone must `pnpm install` / `typecheck` / `build` with no sibling checkout, and the default path must not change (flicker, speed). | `@pmndrs/raytracer` is published: then the wrapper can go (W81's deletion commit ba370e5 is the plan) or stay as the 3-buffer path. | resolved (W89, D5452: published as ^0.1.0; the @pmndrs/bvh wrapper (ba370e5's plan) and the three-mesh-bvh provider are deleted, the raytracer is the one provider) |
| D5301 | probe-core / the one-atlas SSSR fallback + hit shading check pins `hitColorFilter: 'off'` (W82, T71) | The check (W64, D4901) ran the SSSR pass at settings `{ fallbackColor, frameIndex, minTraversalOccupancy }`, i.e. whatever the package default was; the fallback-only and the fallback + hit-shading passes had to agree to one f16 step. | With W76's default cone (`hitColorFilter: 'cone'`, mirror bias compensated) the cone's pipelines compile asynchronously and their variants are cached per device keyed by WGSL, so the two passes (two WGSL variants) ran one cone frame and one 'off' frame: 12403 px differed (probe-core 1 atlas; 4 atlases went through the same). The check now sets `hitColorFilter: 'off'` (the pre-W76 rays it was calibrated on): 0 px differ at 1 and 4 atlases. Not a seam bug: the filter, not the probe fallback, was the difference. | The seam under test is the fallback + re-shade lookup, not the filter; waiting for both variants' cone would need the pass's CONE_READY hook and a longer check. | The check should run at the package default (wait for the cone on both). | open |
## Notes

- **D1/D2 are a pair.** If the core path moves to cube arrays (phase 1 or 2), D2 disappears for that path, but stays for any adapter still on a 2D array.
- D20–D27 come from `docs/research/probe-lineage.md` (R1). D20 and D22 are linked; D21 and D26 close when `projectionOffset` and the world-space helper land.
- D40–D69 come from `docs/research/sources.md` (R2). D62 (default 256 px) conflicts with D23 (we default to 128); the phase 1 bench decides. It has run (256 px halves the mirror error and changes nothing at r > 0, see below), so the choice is open only as a call: default 256, or keep 128 and document 256 for mirrors.
- **Phase 1 bench (W1, `docs/research/probe-layouts.md`)** informs these, without closing them: D1 (raw WebGPU binds `texture_cube_array` fine; `cube-hw` has the lowest error at every mip and is 1.24–1.49× cheaper than `cube2d-warp`), D2 (warp's real cost is a resampled mirror mip, p99 12.7 / 255 at 128 px, plus 1.3–1.5× lookup), D4 (BC6H fails the 2× p99 gate on the roughest mips, 2.7× at r = 1; sampling is 2.5–3× faster when cache-bound), D5 (`octa` fails the gate at the mirror mip; passes on cost), D61 (rg11b10 shows no banding on bright sources under the tone map, but a +0.07–0.19 mean offset; rgb9e5 is better at the same 4 bytes), D62 (256 px halves the mirror error and changes nothing at r > 0), D63/D64 (see D101).
- Entries from the source surveys (`docs/research/sources.md`, `docs/research/probe-lineage.md`) are added here as they are found.
- **Status sweep, 2026-11-11.** Statuses that later entries had overtaken were brought up to date, with the entry that did the work named: D5, D61 (phase 1 decided), D10, D41, D44, D48 (done), D45, D47, D302, D604 (superseded), D7 and D54 (duplicates of D40 and D8), D308 (the per-tile cap and thickness parts are done), D605 (an umbrella over D69 / D1002, D49, D66, D1001). `D2006` had two rows; the first (W18) is marked superseded by the second (W19), and the id stays on the second, which HANDOFF and SPEC refer to.

It replaces `reflectionOut.environmentIrradiance` and Babylon's own irradiance block weights it (base-layer specular energy, clear coat, albedo, `vLightingIntensity.z`, AO); without `scene.environmentTexture` the plugin runs that same block and adds it to `finalAmbient`. `bake()` captures with the set disabled (environment only), so captures stay direct light, as three's first bake. | Matches three's lookup (the reference the engine pages compare against) and keeps the probe maths in core (rule 1). | D5521 | Babylon / glossy march early exit off (W99; D10) | FFX SSSR stops a glossy ray's traversal when its wave has `min_traversal_occupancy` (core default 4) or fewer rays still marching. | The Babylon adapter's `ScreenSpaceReflections` defaults `minTraversalOccupancy` to 0: every glossy ray marches to its end (or `maxTraversalIntersections`). With 0: 0 texels, rest 0.000 / 255. There is no denoiser on Babylon to hide it; three and pixi kept core's 4 (their denoisers average it out) until W101 made 0 core's default (D5560); the Babylon adapter still sets 0 itself, so it stays 0 if core's default changes back. | Revisit when: the Babylon adapter gets the denoiser, or core makes the ray list order deterministic (a sort or per-tile fixed slots). | measured (W99: rest 0.013-0.016 -> 0.000 / 255 over 3 runs, engines numbers unchanged to 0.01 / 255; lab babylon intersect 0.26-0.29 -> 0.21-0.30 ms at 960 x 540, inside the run-to-run noise) |
| D5540 | RT / base colour textures at traced hits (W97; D902, D5204) | A path tracer (and @pmndrs/raytracer's own P1.4c) samples every material texture at the hit's uv, with a per-ray cone for the mip level; three samples the map with screen-space derivatives. | Base colour maps only (`map`, sRGB, uv channel 0, no offset / repeat / rotation, readable on the CPU): three's `bvhTraceSceneFromObjects` decodes each once (RGBA8, a `flipY` image's rows flipped, drawn at most 1024 px a side), adds the meshes' uvs, and core's provider hands them to @pmndrs/raytracer's scene copy (`textures: { maxSize: 1024, maxBuckets: 4 }`, GPU mips); `traceHitMaterial` / `traceHitSpecular` apply `rtApplyMaterialTextures` with a footprint of `t x 0.012` rad x the triangle's uv density (no per-ray cone reaches the seam). Emissive, roughness, metalness and normal maps stay their means (D902); a map with a uv transform keeps its mean. `rtShade` (opt-in `raytracerHitShading`) does not apply textures (raytracer 0.1.0). Each `setTransforms` / `setMaterials` rebuild hands the scene copy the stored level 0 back (a copy, no resample). Option `textures: false` restores the means. | The mean painted the wall behind a moving mesh as a flat grey silhouette of the mesh in the floor's traced reflection (rt/animated, W94's 'grey knot and ball'): MAD vs the same frame without the occluders 22.4 / 23.3 -> 11.8 / 5.8 /255. | D5640 | three / probes / material-side texture limit (W104, T24; D4900) | three.js (#35046, r187) leaves a material past `maxSampledTexturesPerShaderStage` / `maxSamplersPerShaderStage` to WebGPU: its pipeline is rejected ("The number of sampled textures (18) in the Fragment stage exceeds the maximum per-stage limit (16)"), three logs the pipeline error, the mesh does not draw; nothing names the probes. | `ReflectionProbesNode.setup` hooks the NodeBuilder compiling the material (`stageLimits.ts`: its instance's `buildCode`, which three calls once every binding is collected and before any bind group layout or pipeline exists), counts each stage's sampled textures (not storage) and samplers in `builder.bindings`, and warns once per material when one passes the device's limit: the material and mesh names, the count, how many are probe atlases (and their sizes), the limit, and the fix (`reflectionsRequiredLimits(adapter)`, fewer probe sizes or maps; the sampler limit is not raised by it). Replaces the bake's heuristic warning (3+ sizes on a 16-limit device, which also fired when everything fit). Fires at the material's first build: during a bake when `scene.environmentNode` already holds the set (the captures build it), else at the first render. | An exact count is the one WebGPU applies (regression `textureLimit`: ours 18 = WebGPU's 18 at 15 maps + 3 atlases; nothing for a light material on the same set; with `reflectionsRequiredLimits` (32) both draw, no warning). A static count of material maps would miss three's own textures (shadow maps, transmission, the composite's G-buffer inputs). | three gains a public build hook or its own limit message; three stops calling `buildCode` per builder instance (the hook then does nothing; the regression catches it). | measured (`--only regressions`, `textureLimit`; test/material-texture-limit.test.ts) |
| D5680 | SSSR / the cone loses a thin emitter's energy (W106; D5020, D5101, D3550) | Filtered importance sampling (Colbert & Krivanek 2007) prefilters every sample of the lobe by its solid angle, so a small bright source keeps its energy, spread. SSSR 2015's pre-filtered colour pyramid (ours, `hitColorFilter: 'cone'`, the default) applies it only to rays that hit something on screen. | A ray that hits a thin bright object reads it diluted over the cone's footprint (strip / k), and the neighbouring rays that would carry the rest miss (or, with a wall behind, still don't pick it up: cause not pinned down), so the line keeps about 1/k of its energy. X0 lineSpread (bench, e15, raw, AA off): r 0.3 off 107 % of the PT's peak (x1.18 wide) -> cone 2 % (lost), cone scale 0.5 15 %, 0.25 66 %; r 0.15 off 50 % (x1.50) -> cone 19 % (x1.04); lineSpreadThin with a tight thickness (0.01, no alpha term) r 0.3 119 % -> 1 %. | The default's own output does not change: with the upscaler and the FFX denoiser, r 0.3 / 0.5 are lost with the cone off too (W70: they clamp the sparse hits), and the cone is the only config that keeps the r 0.15 line in the width gate (x1.00, docs/research/w106/). Known limitation of the screen-space form, written down so nobody reads "lost at r 0.3" as the denoiser's alone. | A pipeline without the denoiser and the upscaler becomes a target, or the denoiser keeps rare bright samples (D3550's third option: then the cone is what loses the line). Candidate experiment: a miss whose march passed within the cone of on-screen geometry reads the pyramid at its end point. | measured (W106) |
